- Do not use `watchPostEffect` for an effect. It runs again for each value that the effect reads, and an effect of upstream runs again only for the dependencies that it names. A dependency that the effect does not read, such as `restartKey` in `useOpenChangeComplete`, then starts no run.
- An internal component of Base UI that only provides a context and renders its children becomes a composable that the root calls. `LabelableProvider.ts` exports `provideLabelableContext()`.
- An internal root component of Base UI that more than one root part renders also becomes a composable. `useMenuRoot` does the work of `MenuRootInternal` for `Menu.Root` and `Menu.SubmenuRoot`. `useAriaCombobox` does the work of `AriaCombobox`. A root part gives it a view of its props, with one getter for each prop. A spread of the props reads each prop, so each `computed` that reads the view would run again for a change of any prop.
- A value that Base UI reads from the state of another store only in the first render, with `useRefWithInit` or a `useState` initializer, is a constant of `setup` that reads `toRaw` of that state. `useMenuRoot` reads the transition status and the `instantType` of the parent menu of a submenu in this way.
- An internal part of Base UI that public parts wrap also becomes a composable. Its file is a `.ts` file at the mapped path, with the upstream file name. `filter-dropdown/input/FilterDropdownInput.ts` exports `useFilterDropdownInput`.
- The composable returns what the part has of the state, the function ref and the `props` function. The public part gives them to its own `useRenderElement`. A wrapper SFC would have to forward `as`, the `#render` slot, the attributes and `element`.
- Base UI can read the text of children that it does not render, such as the label of an item that the filter hides. A slot is a function that a render must call.
- Do not call a slot in `setup`, in a lifecycle hook or in a watcher. A call in `setup` or in a hook makes Vue warn that the slot runs outside the render function.
- Give the slot to a child component that calls it in its own render and renders nothing. `FilterDropdownHiddenItemText` does this for a hidden item. Vue renders that child again when a value that the slot reads changes.
- Base UI can swap the component of a part for another component through a context, so that a plain menu does not bundle the filter code. `Menu.Popup`, `Menu.List`, `Menu.Group` and `Menu.RadioGroup` do this. The choice is fixed for a mounted part, and Vue runs `setup` one time. So each part stays one SFC with one root node and has no twin.
- The part calls `useMenuFilterImpl()` in `setup`, and an item part calls `useMenuFilterItem()`. The result is `null` in a plain menu. The JSDoc of `menu/filter-root/MenuFilterContext.ts` gives the rest.
- A handler of Base UI can test whether the target of an event is outside its element, because React gives the events of a portal child to that handler. Keep the test as Base UI writes it. In Vue the test is never true for a DOM event of content that `Teleport` moved, because that content sends no event to the element. The `focusin` listener of `useMenuFilterPopup` has such a test. `useMenuFilterPopup` runs the same test for the `focusin` of a popup below that the popup tree gives it, and the target of that event is outside the element.
- A registration that the server HTML needs runs one time in `setup`. Register a getter for the value, so that a later change needs no new registration. Remove the registration in `onScopeDispose`. `useLabel` and `useRegisteredMessageId` do this.
- Do not register with a `watch` that has `immediate` and a cleanup. A server render stops that watcher directly after its first run, and the stop runs the cleanup.
- A registration from which a provider picks one value among many parts runs in `onMounted`. A control that replaces another control then registers after the old control left the registrations. `useLabelableId` does this for the id of a control, with a `watch` for a later change. `useRegisterFieldControl` does it for the control of a field. It registers one getter for all the values of the control. A `watch` registers the same getter again when the `id`, the `name`, the value or `enabled` of the control changes. `useLabelableId` registers the `id` of the control element in `setup` with `useRegisteredId`. That getter gives no `id` before the mount. A control that replaces another control thus gives the `id` of its element after the old control left.
- `Collapsible.Panel` registers its id in `setup` with `useRegisteredId`, and the root takes the id of the last registration. A panel that replaces another panel then registers after it. The root has no panel id while no panel is registered, and an item of an accordion has no trigger id while no trigger is registered. Base UI gives a generated id until the first such part mounts, and that id can be the id of no element (`VUE-80`).
- `Dialog.Title` and `Dialog.Description` register a getter of the `id` of their element in `setup` with `useRegisteredId`. `useDialogInteractions` of `dialog/root/useDialogRoot.ts` makes one registry for each kind. It writes the id of the last registration to `titleElementId` or `descriptionElementId` of the store. Base UI clears that id when any part of the kind unmounts, also when another part of the kind stays.
- `Popover.Title` and `Popover.Description` register their ids in the same way. `Popover.Root` makes the two registries.
- Where Base UI removes a registration in the cleanup of a layout effect, use `onScopeDispose`. It runs while Vue unmounts the component. The `onMounted` of a part that replaces it runs after that.
- A compiled template can mount a new part before it unmounts the part that it replaces. A removal by value then removes the value of the new part.
- Thus register a value that two parts can have by source: a `Map` with one entry for each part. `createIdRegistry()` of `src/internals/labelable-provider` does this for the controls, the labels and the messages, for the controls of a field, for the panels of a collapsible, for the triggers of an accordion item, for the titles and the descriptions of a dialog, of a popover and of a toast, and for the labels of `Menu.Group` and `Menu.RadioGroup`.
- A `#render` slot can give an element with attributes that no prop of the part shows. An effect that depends on such an attribute runs in `onMounted` and `onUpdated`. It compares the values that it reads with the values of its last run, and it stops when they are equal. `AvatarImage.vue` reads the rendered image in this way.
- In a development build, `Menu.SubmenuTrigger` warns in this way about a disabled element of its `#render` slot. The warning comes only one time, so the hooks compare no values.
- The transition status runs on a `flush: 'sync'` watcher. It replaces a state write during render.
- A state that React writes during render, to keep the value of an earlier render, also becomes a `watch` with `flush: 'sync'` and `immediate`. `Field.Error` keeps its last message in this way, for its exit animation.
- A composable that drives `useTransitionStatus` writes its state one time in each run. React batches the writes of an effect, but the sync watcher sees each write. In `useImageLoadingStatus`, a `'loading'` write before a `'loaded'` write would give a loaded image `data-ending-style`.
- `useTransitionStatus` has a fifth parameter, `element`, that upstream does not have. It is the element that gets `data-starting-style`, or a ref or a getter for it. Its JSDoc and its comments give the reason for the read of the layout. Omit `element` only when the element has no enter transition, as `Accordion.Item` does.
- `useUnmountAfterClose` keeps a popup mounted until its exit animation ends. Then it sets `mounted` to `false` and calls `onUnmount` one time. A `forceUnmount()` call while the popup is open is a request. Its JSDoc and its comments give the rules for that request, and for the last element that attached (`VUE-92`).
- Do not decide that request in `nextTick`. With no flush in the queue, `nextTick` runs before the flush of a write that comes later in the same task.
- Vue applies a state write of an animation frame callback in the microtask after that callback. The browser computes the style of the frame after that microtask.
- A transition must sometimes start from a value that an earlier DOM update set. Then read the layout of the element after that update. `useCollapsiblePanel` calls `getBoundingClientRect()` in `nextTick` after it writes the measured size. Without that read, the exit transition does not start.
- React adds `px` to a number in a style object. Vue does not. Write each length that is not zero as a string with its unit, such as `'1px'`.
- Write a style key with a vendor prefix in its CSS form, such as `'-webkit-user-select'`. A server render writes the key `WebkitUserSelect` as `webkit-user-select`, which is not a CSS property.
- Vue writes each key of a style again when the `style` object is not the object of the last render. A temporary inline style that the code writes to the DOM is then lost when the consumer has the same property in `style`.
- Thus keep each temporary declaration that a part writes to its element also in a record that the part renders. Remove the declaration from the record when the code restores the property. `useCollapsiblePanel` returns that record as `temporaryStyle`.
- `CollapsiblePanel.vue` gives `animation-name: none` and the record to `useRenderElement` as `overridingStyle`. They then win over `style` and `stateStyle` of the consumer.
- `getDragStyles()` of `useSwipeDismiss` gives the drag styles at the time of the call. The offset of the drag is not reactive, so a change of the offset renders nothing. A render during a swipe writes the drag styles again. They replace a value that `onProgress` wrote to a movement variable. A `transform` or `transition` key that the part puts after the drag styles replaces the drag in that render.
- `Drawer.Popup` gives the `transform` and the `transition` of the drag to `useRenderElement` as `overridingStyle` while the drawer is swiping. The drag then wins over a `transform` or a `transition` in the `style` of the consumer.
- `Drawer.SwipeArea` writes the movement of an opening gesture and `transition: none` to the popup. During that gesture, `Drawer.Popup` renders the movement that its element holds, and it gives `transition: none` as `overridingStyle`. A render of the popup then keeps the gesture.
- A hidden input that `handleHiddenInputInvalid` grows gets the style constant itself, in a bound object, outside `mergeProps`. That bound object is the only attribute of the input.
- A root can write a temporary declaration to the element of another part. `ScrollAreaRoot.vue` does this for `scroll-snap-type`, while a thumb drag or a track press is active.
- The root saves the inline value that it finds, and it restores that value at the end.
- A render of the other part can replace the declaration. The other part thus calls the root in `onUpdated`, and the root writes the declaration again.
- For a declaration on the element of another part, do not use a record. Vue removes the property when the record loses the declaration. A value that the consumer wrote in script is then lost.
- An attribute key in a props object is the name in Vue's `HTMLAttributes`, such as `tabindex`. `mergeProps` merges by key, so the `tabindex` of a consumer then replaces the `tabindex` of the part.
- With the key `tabIndex`, the merged props hold two keys. Vue then sets one as a DOM property and one as an attribute.
- A parameter of a composable keeps upstream's name, such as `tabIndex` of `useButton`.
- A part that turns autocorrect off gives the key `autoCorrect`, and it takes `autocorrect` out of the attributes of the consumer. The comment in `NumberFieldInput.vue` gives the reason.
- Where upstream takes a native attribute out of its props and does not use it, the part takes the key out of `attrs` in the `props` function of `useRenderElement`. It does not declare a prop for it. `Toggle` does this for `type` and `form`: `const { form, type, ...elementAttrs } = attrs`.
- Where an element can lack the DOM property, a part gives a Boolean attribute the value `true` or `undefined`, never `false`. On an `<svg>`, which has no `hidden` property, Vue writes `false` as `hidden="false"`.
- For `hidden="until-found"`, the props hold the string `'until-found'` as `hidden`. The comments of `useCollapsiblePanel` say what each Vue version writes on a server. They also say why the part writes a plain `hidden` first when the element becomes hidden.
- Base UI adds some listeners to the element of the part with `addEventListener`. Give such a handler in the props of the part. An example is `onBeforematch` of `Collapsible.Panel`. The element of a `#render` slot then gets the listener too. `NavigationMenu.Positioner` is the exception. `usePositioner` takes no props of the part, so the part adds its two capture listeners for the focus in a `watch` of its element. That element is also the element of a `#render` slot.
- A listener that Base UI adds with `{ passive: false }` also goes in the props. An example is the `wheel` listener of `ScrollArea.Scrollbar`. Vue adds the listener of an element with no `passive` option. A browser makes a `wheel` listener passive by default only on the window, the document, the root element and the body.
- A listener that Base UI adds with `{ passive: true }` also goes in the props, with the suffix `Passive` on its key. An example is `onTouchstartPassive` of `Slider.Control`. Vue reads the suffix and adds the listener with that option.
- React adds the listener of `onTouchStart` as a passive listener. A part gives such a handler the suffix `Passive` too: `usePressAndHold` binds `onTouchstartPassive`. Without the suffix, the browser waits for the handler before it starts a scroll.
- React adds the listener of `onTouchMove` as a passive listener also. A composable gives such a listener the suffix `Passive` when the listener calls no `preventDefault()`. `touchProps` of `useSwipeDismiss` has `onTouchstartPassive` and `onTouchmovePassive`.
- `ContextMenu.Trigger` also binds `onTouchstartPassive` and `onTouchmovePassive`. A listener of the consumer stops such a handler with `event.preventVaseUIHandler()` only when it has the `.passive` modifier. A listener with no modifier has another key, so the handler of the part still runs.
- For the rule of Vue for the millisecond of the attach and the focus listeners of a part, see [`useRenderElement` and `useRender`](#userenderelement-and-userender). `addInputFocusListeners` in `filter-dropdown/input/FilterDropdownInput.ts` adds the focus listeners of an input with `addEventListener` in its function ref. Its JSDoc gives the two reasons. `useFilterDropdownInput`, `Menu.Input` and `Combobox.Input` call it.
- An `<input>` part gives an uncontrolled default value with the key `value` on a server and with the key `defaultValue` in a browser. A server needs the key `value` to write the `value` attribute. In a browser, Vue sets the DOM property `value` again on each render, and that replaces the text that the user typed. The DOM property `defaultValue` is the `value` attribute, and Vue sets it only when its value changes. The key `^value` does not help, because a server of Vue 3.5.12 writes the attribute `^value`. `Toolbar.Input` and `Field.Control` are the examples.
- A controlled `<input>` part writes the value of its prop to the input in `nextTick` after an event, when the two differ. Vue writes the DOM value only when the part renders. The part does not render when a listener cancels the change or the consumer keeps its value. React writes the prop back after each event. `Field.Control` is the example. The hidden input of `OTPField.Root` does this for the value of the root, also when the root is not controlled.
- The wait for that write starts after the change that the event makes. A wait that starts before the change can end before the flush of a parent that takes the new value. The part then writes the old value to the input. A slot of `OTPField.Input` keeps this order for its character.
- A text input part can refuse a change. `NumberField.Input` is an example. No render follows a refused change, so the part writes the text of its state to the input in the `input` handler. The part does not write while the event has `isComposing`, because a write stops the composition. After `compositionend` it writes in a 0 ms timeout, because WebKit sends the last `input` event of a composition after `compositionend`.
- A text input part listens to `input` and to `change`. It handles `change` only when the value of the input is new. A value is not new when the input had it after the last event that the part handled. A value is also not new when the input had it after the last write of Vue, of the part or of a reset. React's `onChange` does the same, because its value tracker also follows the writes of React. A program that fills a form can send only `change`. A range input follows the same rule, as the input of `Slider.Thumb` does. At the mount, `Combobox.Input` records the text of its state after the value sanitization of its element (`VUE-102`). The browser removes the line breaks of that text from an input.
- Vue writes `value` of an input when it hydrates. The range input of `Slider.Thumb` thus keeps a value of before the hydration with `useHiddenValueBeforeHydration`. It takes a value that the browser restores after the mount with `useFormRestoreAsInput`. See [Parts with a hidden input](#parts-with-a-hidden-input).
- React 19 does not write the `value` or the `checked` of an input when it hydrates, and it calls no `onChange`. Vue writes the `value`. A part thus takes the text or the state of an input that changed before the hydration, after the flush of the mount. A text input gets one `input` event. [Server render](#server-render) and [Parts with a hidden input](#parts-with-a-hidden-input) give the rules. The JSDoc of `src/common/hydration.ts` gives the events of a take.
- The listener of the element itself gets each of these events. A Vue listener of an ancestor can skip one, because of the rule for the millisecond of the attach in [`useRenderElement` and `useRender`](#userenderelement-and-userender).
- A native reset of a form changes the value of an input and sends no `input` event. A part that holds state for that value follows the reset with `useFormReset` of `src/internals`. It clears the error of its field with the function that `useClearFieldError` returns. [Parts with a hidden input](#parts-with-a-hidden-input) gives the rules. `Field.Control` is the example.
- Base UI can decide in a microtask that runs after the handlers of the ancestors. React runs all its handlers in one native listener, so its microtask comes after them. For a real key press, the browser runs the microtasks after each listener. Use a 0 ms timeout. `Checkbox.Root` does this for Enter. Its handler keeps the default prevented, and it gives the event own properties `defaultPrevented` and `preventDefault` until the timeout. A listener of an ancestor then reads `false`, and it can still prevent the submit.
- A teleported child popup has four links to its parent: the popup tree and the `provide` chains of `useDismiss`, `useListNavigation` and `useFloatingFocusManager`. See [Popup dismissal](#popup-dismissal), [Popup list navigation](#popup-list-navigation) and [Focus helpers](#focus-helpers).
- A submenu popup below a filterable root gives its `focusin` to the popups above through the popup tree. For a filterable parent, it also gives its `keydown`. A part in a popup listens with `useNestedPopupEventListener` in `setup`. The JSDoc of `menu/filter-root/nestedPopupEvents.ts` and of `MenuFilterContext.ts` gives the events and the cases.
- An outside press and a focus-out treat the popup of a descendant node as inside.
- `KeepAlive` is the counterpart of `React.Activity`. Under `KeepAlive`, a part keeps its state. In `onDeactivated` it releases the scroll lock, the modal marks, the document listeners and the timers. In `onActivated` it takes them back.
- A dialog reports its open state to the dialog above it. While `KeepAlive` holds the dialog, it reports that it is closed. The dialog above is then the top dialog again, so Escape closes it. The report comes back at the activation.
- A drawer in a `Drawer.Provider` reports its open state to the provider, and a drawer can hold a close watcher. While `KeepAlive` holds the drawer, it reports to the provider that it is closed, and it destroys its close watcher. Both come back at the activation.
- A nested drawer reports the height and the presence of its popup to the drawer above it. While `KeepAlive` holds the nested drawer, its popup reports a height of 0 and no presence. Both come back at the activation.
- A part that clears a timer in `onDeactivated` also ends the state that the timer would end. `ScrollAreaRoot.vue` ends its scrolling state.
- A part that follows a pointer ends that state in `onDeactivated`, because `KeepAlive` sends no leave event. `ScrollAreaRoot.vue` does this for its hover state.
- A swipe in progress ends with no dismissal in `onDeactivated`. The browser releases the pointer capture of an element that leaves the document, so no `pointerup` comes to the element. `useSwipeDismiss` does this.
- `useSwipeDismiss` calls no `onCancel` in `onDeactivated`. A part that keeps a swipe state of its own thus ends that state in its own `onDeactivated` hook. `Drawer.SwipeArea` removes its swipe styles from the popup and the backdrop, and it lets an outside press close the drawer again. It does the same when it unmounts during a gesture, because no release comes then (`VUE-57`).
- A part that reads `:hover` at mount reads it again in `onActivated`. No enter event comes for a pointer that is already over the part. `ScrollAreaViewport.vue` does this. A part that mounted in a held branch reads it at its first activation.
- An effect that reads the DOM stops while the part is deactivated. `onDeactivated` runs the cleanup of the effect, and `onActivated` runs the effect again. `useCollapsiblePanel` does this for its size effect and its close effect.
- `useUnmountAfterClose` is the exception. A close that is in progress when `KeepAlive` deactivates the root completes, and `onUnmount` runs one time. A wait that starts again at the activation would play a keyframe exit a second time.
- `onActivated` also runs at the first mount in a `KeepAlive`. Keep a flag that `onDeactivated` sets. Do nothing in `onActivated` while the flag is not set.
- A part that mounts in a branch that `KeepAlive` holds gets no `onDeactivated` call. Its first `onActivated` call comes with no `onDeactivated` call before it, so the flag of the rule above is not set at that call. `useKeepAliveState()` of `src/internals` starts `isHeld` with `isInHeldBranch()` in `setup`. It ignores the call in the flush of the mount only when the part mounts with its branch. `shown` counts each other activation. An app that mounts in a detached element is not held, because no `KeepAlive` holds it.
- `useLabel` registers a label with the labelable context. With `registerLabelId`, it also registers the label with a root of its own.
- A part whose own flag stops an effect that reaches out of the branch starts that flag with `isInHeldBranch()` of `src/internals/useKeepAliveState.ts`. The function reads `isDeactivated` of the part and of the components above it, as Vue does before it calls a `KeepAlive` hook. `useFloatingPortalNode`, `useDomEffect`, `useScrollLock`, `useDismiss`, `useFloatingFocusManager`, `useDialogRoot`, `DrawerRoot.vue`, `DrawerPopup.vue` and `DrawerViewport.vue` do this. A popup that mounts in a held branch thus stays out of the document until the activation. Until then it takes no scroll lock, no modal marks and no focus, and it does not close on a press outside it. It reports no open state to a dialog or a drawer above it, or to `Drawer.Provider`. At the activation, it takes the place that a popup that mounts in a shown branch has (`VUE-5`). `useClientPoint` also starts the flag of its window listener with `isInHeldBranch()`. `ToastRoot.vue` and `usePopupAutoResize` do the same, because a read of the layout in a held branch gives 0. `ResizablePanel.vue` and `ResizableHandle.vue` start the flag of their registration with the root in this way, so a root gives no room to a panel that is not in the document. `ResizableRoot.vue` starts its own flag in this way too. It takes the panels of its first activation as the panels of its mount, so that activation emits no change. `useKeepAliveState`, `useFloatingNodeId`, `Toast.Provider` and `useFormRestore` also start their flags with `isInHeldBranch()`. The node of a popup that mounts in a held branch counts as open only from the activation, so a popup above it closes on Escape. A provider that mounts in a held branch starts no timer of a toast until the activation. `useFormRestore` adds no `pageshow` listener until the activation.
- A flag whose effect no user meets while `KeepAlive` holds the part is the exception to the rule above, and it starts `false`. `ContextMenu.Trigger`, `useFocus`, `Toast.Viewport`, `useAnchorPositioning`, the timers of `Select.Trigger` and `useMenuRoot`, `NavigationMenu.Positioner`, the trigger registration, `useImplicitActiveTrigger` and the observers of `useCollapsiblePanel`, `Tabs.List`, `Slider.Thumb`, `ScrollArea.Content` and `Toast.Content` do this.
- The handle attach of `usePopupRoot` is also an exception. [Popup store](#popup-store) gives its rule.
- A part whose `onActivated` does work that a user meets at the show, such as a read of `:hover`, also starts its flag with `isInHeldBranch()`. `NavigationMenuTrigger.vue` and `ScrollAreaViewport.vue` do this.
- A part that mounts in the flush of an activation also starts with the flag set. Vue clears `isDeactivated` after the renders of that flush, and the part then gets `onActivated` in the same flush. That call can come before `onMounted`, and it must clear the flag. Do not make it wait for the mount.
- `useKeepAliveState` reads `isInHeldBranch()` in `setup` to find a part that mounts in the flush of an activation. The order of `onActivated` and `onMounted` cannot show such a part, because that order changes. The part gets `onActivated` first when a component of the branch mounts the part from its own state. It gets `onMounted` first when the root of the branch gets the part from a prop. `KeepAlive` then mounts the part in its patch of the root, before it queues the hooks of the show.
- Where the effect is a `watch` with `onCleanup`, the flag is a `shallowRef` in the sources of that `watch`. The callback returns while the flag is set. `ScrollAreaViewport.vue` and `ScrollAreaContent.vue` do this for their observers.
- A listener or an observer on the root node of an element of the part reads that node again at each activation, with `shown` of `useKeepAliveState` in the sources of its `watch`. `useAriaLabelledBy`, `useFormReset` and `useFormEntryValue` do this. Until its first activation, a part that mounts in a held branch has the detached element of `KeepAlive` as its root node. The activation calls no `ref` setter. An observer that moves to the new root node after each move of the element needs no `shown`. The observer of the detached element sees the first activation of a part that mounts in a held branch. `isHeld` in the sources of the `watch` starts the observer again at each other activation. `useFieldsetDisabled` does this.
- `KeepAlive` moves a branch out of the document before it calls `onDeactivated`. An effect that a change queued earlier in the same flush can run in that gap. A read of the layout of an element of the branch then gives 0 or an empty value. An effect that runs again at the activation and only writes a measured value can run in the gap. An effect that keeps a choice from such a read returns while the element is not in the document (`isConnected`). `SelectPopup.vue` does this for the trigger in its placement. Its popup is in a `Teleport` and stays in the document in that gap.
- `KeepAlive` takes the DOM of a deactivated part out of the document. The id of that part then must not stay in a `for`, an `aria-controls`, an `aria-owns`, an `aria-labelledby` or an `aria-describedby`.
- A registration with a provider registers a getter one time and keeps its entry and its place. The getter gives no value while `KeepAlive` holds the part. `useRegisteredId` does this for a label, a message and the panel of a collapsible, and `useLabelableId` does it for the id of a control. `useRegisterFieldControl` does it for the control of a field, with one difference. It registers the getter again when a value of the control changes, and the entry then goes to the end of the registry.
- The getter of such a registration reads `isHeld` of `useKeepAliveState`. `KeepAlive` runs the `onActivated` hooks of one branch from the last part to the first part. A new entry in that hook would reverse the order of the parts.
- `useRegisteredLabelId` gives one id to the root, not a getter. A label of the first render of the root gives its id in `setup`, for the server HTML and the hydration. A label that mounts later gives its id in `onMounted`, and only when it is not held.
- A `watch` of `isHeld` and the id removes the id when `KeepAlive` holds the label, and only when the root still has that id.
- At each activation, the label queues itself with the root in a `watch` of `shown`. A label that mounts in the flush of the activation also queues itself. The root names the last queued label in document order, one time for each flush. React runs the effects of a branch that `Activity` shows in tree order, so the last label of the branch wins there too.
- The `watch` of `shown` has `flush: 'sync'`. With the default flush, the root would pick a label between the watchers of two labels.
- A pick of one value from many registrations, such as the control id of a provider, runs in a `watch` with the default flush. The pick runs one time for each flush, against all registrations, as the state updater of Base UI does. It then does not depend on the order of the hooks. A pick that keeps no earlier value, such as `controlElementId` of the labelable context, is a `computed`. It also reads all the registrations together.
- An action of a part that needs the registrations of other parts runs in a `watch` of `shown`. In its own hook, the parts before it are still held and give no value. `useLabelableId` does this for a control with no id: it takes the default id of the provider again.
- The active control of a field is the last control of its registry that gives a registration. A control registers again when its `id`, its `name`, its value or `enabled` changes, as the effect of Base UI does. When the active control goes away, the field uses the control that was active before it and is still there. Base UI has no control then.
- A field gives no value to its form while it has no active control. `fields` of the form context then does not list the field, so a form does not validate it, submit its value or focus its control. A form finds the first invalid control by the position of the controls in the document, as Base UI does.
- A control with one input gives that input to its field with `useFieldInput()` of `src/internals/field-register-control`. It does not write `validation.inputRef`. It does not use `validation.inputRef` to know whether `KeepAlive` holds it, because that ref can name the input of a held control. When the last input goes, the field validates the input before it that is still there (`VUE-83`). The JSDoc and the comments of `useRegisterFieldControl` and `useFieldInput` give the rules under `KeepAlive` and the sequence of the registrations.
- The registry of a composite is different: it holds elements and sorts them by their position in the document. See [Composite](#composite).
- Do not use the Vue `<Transition>` component. CSS drives animation through `data-starting-style`, `data-ending-style` and `getAnimations()`, as upstream.
- Give `useFloating` an element or a virtual element, never a component. It reads the element of a component through `$el`. `resolveElement` changes a component into an element.
- The reference of `useFloating` is `null` before the mount, while the popup is not mounted, and from a deactivation by `KeepAlive` to the next activation. `useFloating` then starts no `autoUpdate` and computes no position. A popup that mounts in a held branch starts `autoUpdate` at its mount. Its passes measure elements out of the document, and the first pass after the activation gives the position of a popup that mounts in a shown branch.
- Write the anchor to a `shallowRef` that the reference reads, in a `watch` with `flush: 'post'`. A getter of the consumer can read the DOM of the render.
- `useAnchorPositioning` is the only caller of `useFloating`. Its comments give the reasons for the `computed` of the reference, for its options and for the flush of each watcher.
- A key that no rendered style has, such as `--anchor-width`, keeps the value that code writes on the element. A value that code writes for a key of the rendered style goes into a ref that the style reads, such as `--available-height`.
- Some values change in each frame of a gesture, such as `--drawer-swipe-progress` of a swipe. Do not keep such a value in a ref, because a ref renders the part in each frame. Where the rendered style has the key of such a value, the style reads the value from a holder that is not reactive. `Drawer.Indent` reads the visual state store of `Drawer.Provider`. `Drawer.Backdrop` reads the value that its element holds. `Drawer.Popup` reads the nested swipe progress store of `Drawer.Root`.
- A function ref of a composable reads no ref in the render, because the render then depends on that ref. Where the function ref must start work that depends on a ref, it reads the ref in a `queueMicrotask` callback. `arrowRef` of `useAnchorPositioning` does this.
- The check for a rectangle must refuse a component. The ref of a component with no `expose` also gives its props. A component that has `width` and `height` props then has the names of a rectangle. A rectangle has no `nodeType` and no `$props`, and its `x`, `y`, `width` and `height` are numbers.

### What stays from Base UI

- Use the ports of upstream's `useTimeout` and `useAnimationFrame`. Do not call `setTimeout` or `requestAnimationFrame` directly.
- Use the shadow DOM-safe helpers for DOM traversal and event targets: `closest`, `contains`, `getTarget` and `activeElement`.
- Write development checks as `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'`.

### Error messages

Each error message of the package says three things:

1. What occurred.
2. Why it is a problem.
3. How to correct it.

Example:

```text
Vase UI: <Dialog.Trigger> must be inside <Dialog.Root>. Without a root the trigger has no dialog to open. Put the trigger inside <Dialog.Root>, or pass a `handle`.
```

### Comments

- The code is the documentation. Correct a name or the structure before you add a comment.
- A comment stays only where it gives a reason that the code cannot show.
- Do not write the history of the code in a comment.
- Write the reason for a node of a template as a comment in the script block. A comment in a template becomes a DOM node in a development build.

## Tests

### Where a test runs

- A test file runs in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.
- `packages/vue/vitest.config.ts` sets the Firefox preference `dom.multiple_import_maps.enabled` to `false`. Vitest sets it to `true`, and Firefox then sometimes crashes a page when the page starts.
- With `false`, Vitest mocks a module in Firefox by request interception, not by import map. Do not set `browser.importMapMocks` to `true`: Vitest then refuses a mock in Firefox.
- `packages/vue/vitest.config.ts` also sets the Firefox preference `dom.w3c_touch_events.enabled` to `1`. Without it, a test in Firefox has no `Touch` and no `TouchEvent`.
- `packages/vue/vitest.config.ts` runs the test files of Firefox one at a time. Firefox has one mouse for all its windows, and each test file that runs at the same time gets a window of its own. When the real pointer leaves the page of one window, Firefox can send the leave events to the page of another window. The first page then keeps the pointer, and the other page gets `mouseleave` events that its test did not cause.
- The job `test-browser` of CI runs the Firefox tests in three shards: `--shard=1/3`, `--shard=2/3` and `--shard=3/3`. Each shard is a job on its own runner, so the shards do not share a mouse. To run one shard on your machine, add its flag: `pnpm test:firefox --shard=1/3`. With no `--shard`, `pnpm test:firefox` runs all test files.
- The job `test-browser` also runs the WebKit tests in three shards, with the same flags. A WebKit page keeps the memory of each finished test file, because of a fault in the inspector of WebKit, so one job for all files runs out of memory. Add a shard when the files of a shard pass about 400.
- On a pull request, the job `select` runs `scripts/ci/selectTests.ts` on the changed files. The script tells `test-nuxt` whether to run. It also tells the test jobs `test-node`, `test-browser`, `test-vue-floor` and `test-vue-rc` which test files of `packages/vue` to run.
- The merge queue skips `select`. Then each job runs, and each test job runs each test file.
- `check` and `docs-axe` run for each pull request. They read the whole repository.
- A change of one of these files runs `test-nuxt` and each test file:
  - `.github`, `scripts/ci`, each `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `patches` and `.node-version`.
  - Each `tsconfig` file and `packages/vue/vitest.config.ts`.
  - `packages/vue/test/server`, each shell script in `packages/vue/test`, and `parity/own`.
  - A file that the script does not name.
- A pull request with only the files below and no file of the list above runs no test job and no `test-nuxt`. No test of `packages/vue` and no fixture reads them:
  - The Markdown files of the root and of `docs`, `LICENSE` and `docs/public`.
  - `parity` and `scripts`, except `parity/own` and `scripts/ci`.
  - `eslint.config.js`, `eslint.config.test.js` and `prettier.config.js`.
- `test-nuxt` runs for a file of `fixtures` and for a file of `packages/vue` that the build can read. The build does not read `packages/vue/test`, or a file with `.test` or `.spec` before its extension. No test job reads `fixtures`.
- For each other changed file of `packages/vue/src`, `packages/vue/test`, `packages/vue/scripts` or `docs`, a test job runs `vitest related`. Vitest runs the test files that import the changed file, directly or through other modules.
- `test-nuxt` and each test file also run when the list for `vitest related` has more than 500 paths. They also run when a path of the list has a character other than `A`–`Z`, `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `_`, `@`, `.`, `+`, `/` and `-`.
- Vitest does not know a file that a test reads by a path at run time, such as the `file` of `renderOnServer`. The script thus adds the test files in the folder of each changed file of `packages/vue/src` and `packages/vue/test`. It also adds the test files in each folder above it, up to `src` or `test`. A changed test file adds none.
- Keep a file that a test reads by a path, and does not import, in the folder of the test or below it. For a file elsewhere, add its path to the list of files in `scripts/ci/selectTests.ts` that run each test file.
- A deleted file of `docs` runs each test file, unless it is a Markdown file or a file of `docs/public`. Test files of `packages/vue/test`, such as `demos.test.tsx`, read the folders of the demos with `import.meta.glob`. The module graph does not hold a deleted file.
- `required` fails when a test job or `test-nuxt` is skipped and `select` did not say that it has nothing to run.
- CI does not run the scripts `test:node`, `test:chromium` and the others. The action `.github/actions/vitest` runs `vitest` with the same project names.
- Set the `timeout-minutes` of a CI job to 1.5 times the 95th percentile of the time of its passed runs. Use the runs of the merge queue, not of a pull request. A job of a pull request can run only some of the test files. If the result is not a multiple of 5 minutes, increase it to the next multiple. For a job with a matrix, use the slowest entry. When a passed run takes more than two thirds of the limit of its job, measure the job again.
- On macOS, `packages/vue/vitest.config.ts` starts WebKit through `test/webkitFullKeyboardAccess.sh`. The script gives the WebKit process the arguments `-AppleKeyboardUIMode 2`, which set full keyboard access for that process. macOS has full keyboard access off by default. Without it, the Tab key of WebKit on macOS skips a button or a checkbox. WebKit on Linux, Chromium and Firefox do not skip them.
- Playwright refuses a launch argument that does not start with `-`, so the value `2` goes through the script.
- Full keyboard access does not put a link in the order of the Tab key. A case that moves the focus to a link with the Tab key thus fails in WebKit on macOS.
- On Linux, Chromium keeps a block of shared memory for each module that a new test frame loads again, until a garbage collection frees it. A page that holds about 16,000 blocks loses its connections. Vitest then reports `Browser connection was closed`, and the job fails with no failed case.
- On Linux, Vitest collects the garbage of Chromium after a test file only when the temporary folder has less free space than `VITEST_CHROMIUM_GC_DISK_THRESHOLD_GB` gives, in GB. The default is 4, and a CI runner has about 85 GB free. CI sets the variable to `1000000`, so Vitest collects the garbage after each test file. Firefox and WebKit ignore the variable.
- In Firefox, `test/setup.ts` runs the browser command `collectGarbage` before the first test of each file. The command calls `page.requestGC()` of Playwright, which runs a full garbage collection and a cycle collection in Firefox. Without it, Firefox collects the garbage of the earlier test files of the page late. One cycle collection then visits millions of objects and stops the page for some seconds. A test that runs at that time takes those seconds.
- On CI, each test file of a WebKit job adds about 23 MB to the used memory of the runner until the job ends. A WebKit job of 520 test files thus uses about 14 GB of the 16 GB of its runner. `collectGarbage` does not free this memory: while Playwright is attached, the inspector of WebKit keeps the global object of the removed frame of each file.
- Vitest keeps the modules that it transforms for Node in `node_modules/.vitest-cache` at the root of the repository, and the next run uses them again. These are the modules of the Node tests and the modules that `renderOnServer` imports. A CI job starts with no such folder, so it transforms each module. To measure the time of a test as CI does, add `--no-fsModuleCache`: `pnpm test:webkit --no-fsModuleCache`.
- The project `vapor` runs the browser tests in Chromium in a Vapor app. `createTestApp` of `test/testApp.ts` makes the app with `createVaporApp` and `vaporInteropPlugin`. The root of the app is the Vapor component `test/VaporRoot.vue`.
- The project needs Vue 3.6, and the package develops on Vue 3.5. `pnpm test` thus leaves the project out. The job `test-vue-rc` installs the release candidate and runs the project `vapor`.
- To run the project on your machine, install the Vue version of that job: `pnpm --filter @vase-ui/vue add --save-dev --save-exact vue@<version>`. After the run, restore `packages/vue/package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` with git. Then run `pnpm install`. Do not commit the release candidate.
- The hydration helpers and a test that makes its own app use an ordinary Vue app in each project.
- A test file with a name that ends in `.node.test.ts` or `.node.test.tsx` runs in Node with no DOM. Use it for server render and for logic that must work with no DOM.
- A `.node.test` file does not import `#test-utils`, because that index loads the libraries of the DOM helpers. To render a part, such a file imports `renderToString` from `vue/server-renderer` and the part directly.
- A family can have one `.node.test.tsx` file for the server render of the whole family, in the folder of its root: `progress/root/ProgressRoot.node.test.tsx`. A `replaced` Base UI case is the exception. It goes to the `.node.test.tsx` file of the folder of its Base UI test, as [How to port a test](#how-to-port-a-test) says: `fieldset/legend/FieldsetLegend.node.test.tsx`.
- There is no jsdom.
- A file with a name that ends in `.spec.ts`, `.spec.tsx` or `.spec.vue` is a type test. vue-tsc checks it, and no test runner runs it.
- A `.spec.vue` file is the type test of a template. Its first line is `<!-- @strictTemplates true -->`. vue-tsc then reports an unknown prop in that file, as it does for a consumer that sets `strictTemplates`.
- In this package, vue-tsc reports no unknown prop on a generic SFC, in a `.spec.vue` file also. The files that are not strict give each generic SFC a type that accepts each unknown prop. For such a part, write a line that must fail: `<!-- @vue-expect-error -->` before an element that gives the prop a value of a wrong type.
- The tests of `fixtures/nuxt` run in Node. They start the Nuxt app with `@nuxt/test-utils` and read its pages in Chromium. They are not part of `pnpm test`. The job `test-nuxt` of CI runs them, and `required` needs that job.
- The cases of the page `form-state` also run in Firefox and WebKit, because each engine restores the form state of a page at another time. Each case launches its engine with `playwright-core` against the one server. The job `test-nuxt` installs the three browsers.
- The test of `fixtures/vite` builds a client app that mounts in its entry module, and reads it in Chromium. Chromium restores the form state of that page after the mount, so the test reaches the `pageshow` listeners of the parts. The job `test-nuxt` runs it with `pnpm test:vite`.
- Firefox and WebKit restore no input of that page, because the app makes its inputs after the parse. Chromium saves the value of a text, a number or a range input only when the value differs from its `value` attribute. It saves a `date` or a `time` input also when the two are equal. Vue writes that attribute with each value of a part. The test thus writes the inputs of the slider with a script before it leaves the page, and the visitor types the date.
- In that test, the thumbs and the date field follow the size of the radio group. The take of the group thus renders them before their own `pageshow` listeners run.
- The job `test-nuxt` also runs `pnpm --filter @vase-ui/nuxt-fixture typecheck`. vue-tsc then checks the templates of the fixture against the built package, as it does in the project of a consumer.

### How to port a test

A ported test is a file at the mapped path with the same `describe` and `it` titles as upstream. `pnpm parity:check` finds the ported case of a Base UI case by these titles. It does not read the body of a case.

- A ported case asserts each behavior that the Base UI case asserts. It can assert more.
- The body of a ported case can have a better form than the body of the Base UI case. A better form never asserts less.
- Examples are a wait for an event with `nextEvent` in place of a `waitFor` on a flag, and a direct assertion in place of a negative assertion in `waitFor`. More examples are a real event of the browser in place of a dispatched event, and a stricter assertion. See [Test rules](#test-rules).
- State each such change in the body of the pull request.
- Only a rule of this section or an exception row removes a Base UI assertion or makes it less strict. An example is the rule for a warning that only React DOM gives.

Where a case has no better form, it differs from the Base UI case only by these rules:

| Base UI test                                                                                               | Vase UI test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `from '@base-ui/react/<entry>'`                                                                            | `from '@vase-ui/vue/<entry>'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `from '@base-ui/react'`                                                                                    | `from '@vase-ui/vue'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `from '@base-ui/utils/<file>'`                                                                             | A relative path to `src/common/<file>`                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `vi.mock('@base-ui/utils/<file>', …)` and `vi.importActual` of that module                                 | The same calls with the relative path to `src/common/<file>`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `from '@mui/internal-test-utils'`                                                                          | `from '#test-utils'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `userEvent` of `@testing-library/user-event`, imported in the test                                         | `user` of the `render` result: `const { user } = await render(…)`, then `await user.click(…)`.                                                                                                                                                            |
| A read of a `Menu`, `Select` or `Combobox` popup, or a press, directly after `await user.click(trigger)`   | To read, `await screen.findByRole…`. To press, give the open to `waitForLaterMillisecond`. The popup opens a frame after `mousedown`; `user.click` can resolve first. To expect no popup, call `await waitSingleFrame()`, then `await flushMicrotasks()`. |
| `from '@floating-ui/react-dom'`                                                                            | `from '@floating-ui/vue'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A global name with `BASE_UI`, such as `BASE_UI_ANIMATIONS_DISABLED`                                        | The Vase UI name, such as `VASE_UI_ANIMATIONS_DISABLED`. See [Layout and names](#layout-and-names).                                                                                                                                                       |
| `event.preventBaseUIHandler()`                                                                             | `event.preventVaseUIHandler()`                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `className`                                                                                                | `class`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `tabIndex` as a prop or as a key of a props object                                                         | `tabindex`. An assertion on the DOM property keeps `tabIndex`.                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `noValidate` as a prop or as a key of a props object                                                       | `novalidate`. An assertion on the DOM property keeps `noValidate`.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `crossOrigin`, `referrerPolicy` or `srcSet` as a prop or as a key of a props object                        | `crossorigin`, `referrerpolicy` or `srcset`. A DOM property keeps its name.                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `minLength` or `readOnly` as a prop or as a key of a props object                                          | `minlength` or `readonly`. A DOM property keeps its name.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `autoFocus` that gives the focus to an element before the case acts                                        | A `focus()` call after `render`, or in `onMounted` for an element that mounts later. React focuses such an element when it mounts. Vue does not, and the browser can ignore `autofocus` on an element that a script adds.                                 |
| `render={<x />}`, an element with no props                                                                 | `as`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `render={<x a={b} />}`, an element with props of its own                                                   | `v-slots={{ render }}`. The function returns `<x {...props} a={b} />`, with the own props after the spread.                                                                                                                                               |
| A render function                                                                                          | `v-slots={{ render }}`. Give its parameter a type. Move the children of the part into the element that it returns.                                                                                                                                        |
| A case that expects a part to read an attribute of its `render` element, such as an `aria-label`           | No counterpart. A part does not read the attributes of the element that its `#render` slot returns, other than an `id` that another part names. Give the case a `not-applicable` row. The attribute on the part gives the same result.                    |
| A case that expects another part to follow the `id` of a `render` element, such as the `id` of a list      | `v-slots={{ render }}` with the `id` after the spread. The part, its store or its root reads the `id` of the element. See [Popup store](#popup-store).                                                                                                    |
| A function as children: `<Part>{(args) => …}</Part>`                                                       | `v-slots={{ default: (args: Type) => … }}`                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A handler key of a DOM event, such as `onKeyDown`                                                          | The Vue key, such as `onKeydown`. See [DOM event names](#dom-event-names).                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A `Capture` listener on an element, such as `onClickCapture={fn}`                                          | A spread: `{...{ onClickCapture: fn }}`                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `{ nativeEvent: new MouseEvent('click') }`                                                                 | `new MouseEvent('click')`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `ref` on the outermost vnode, or on a child of a plain element                                             | A function for `render`: `render(() => <Button ref={ref} />)`                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| A `Wrapper` that forwards a ref                                                                            | A `Wrapper` that exposes `element`. A wrapper that can render no element sets `element` with a function ref.                                                                                                                                              |
| `React.createRef()` and `ref.current`                                                                      | A function ref that writes a variable, or a `shallowRef`. A ref on a part gives the component, so read its `element`.                                                                                                                                     |
| `inputRef={ref}`                                                                                           | A ref on the part. Read `inputElement` of the component.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| A `ref` callback on a part that acts on the element                                                        | The `#render` slot with a function ref on its element. The function calls `props.ref` first, and it acts one time. Vue calls a function ref again on each patch. `actOnElement` of `test/fixtures/actOnElement.ts` makes this slot.                       |
| `{ current: value }` as a ref object                                                                       | `shallowRef(value)`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `actionsRef` and `actionsRef.current?.validate()`                                                          | A function ref on the root in a function for `render`, then `root.validate()`                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `useMergedRefs(a, b)`                                                                                      | A function ref that calls each ref                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| A component that keeps the first ref callback that it got, such as `StaleRefButton`                        | No counterpart. A part gives its ref callbacks one time, in `setup`. Give the case a `not-applicable` row.                                                                                                                                                |
| `React.useState` in a test component                                                                       | A `shallowRef` in the setup function of `defineComponent`                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A forced render of the parent that must render the part again, such as `forceRender` of `React.useReducer` | A `shallowRef` count that the test binds to an attribute of the part, such as `data-render`                                                                                                                                                               |
| `React.memo` around a test component, so that a render of its parent does not render it again              | A component with no props and no children. Vue renders a child component again only when its props, its slots or the reactive values that its render reads change.                                                                                        |
| `<React.Fragment key={x}>` around a tree that must mount again when `x` changes                            | `<Fragment key={x}>` of `vue`. A change of the key mounts the tree again.                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `setProps({ children: <Part … /> })`                                                                       | A `shallowRef` that the test component reads                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `useIsoLayoutEffect` or `React.useEffect` in a test component                                              | `onMounted`. Where a dependency changes in the test, add the `watch` of [What differs from the React source](#what-differs-from-the-react-source).                                                                                                        |
| A layout effect of a parent that reads what the layout effect of a child wrote                             | `onMounted` and `onUpdated` of the parent. The parent reads the dependency in its own render function. A `watch` with `flush: 'post'` runs before the `onMounted` hook of a child that mounts in the same flush.                                          |
| A case that asserts that a part acts before the passive effects of React run                               | A timer that a test component sets in `onMounted`, or that the case sets before the change. The case asserts that the part acted before the timer ran. Vue runs the hooks of a mount and of an unmount in the flush, before the next task.                |
| A counter of renders that `React.useEffect` raises in a test component                                     | A counter that `onUpdated` raises. It counts no render of the mount, so the case asserts the numbers of Vue and not the numbers of React.                                                                                                                 |
| A component in `render` that counts its renders, such as `LoggingRoot`                                     | A function of the `#render` slot that counts its calls. Vue calls it in each render of the part, one time. React renders two times under Strict Mode, so the case asserts the numbers of Vue.                                                             |
| A count of renders that starts after `render`, for the positioner of a popup                               | `await waitForPositioned(positioner)` before the count starts. The first position pass of the popup renders the positioner again. It does not render the parts below the positioner.                                                                      |
| `useStableCallback(fn)`                                                                                    | `fn`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `autoFocus` on an element                                                                                  | A function ref that focuses the element. React focuses such an element at the mount. A browser focuses only the first `autofocus` element of a document.                                                                                                  |
| `element.focus()` in a case that reads the scroll position of a container                                  | `element.focus({ preventScroll: true })`. Chromium scrolls the element into view in the call. Firefox and WebKit can scroll it later.                                                                                                                     |
| A `user.click` on an input, before a case sets the scroll position of a container that holds the input     | `await waitSingleFrame()` after the click. In the frame after the click, Firefox can scroll that container again. That scroll replaces the position that the case sets. The helper waits for one animation frame.                                         |
| A case that compares the result of `React.useCallback` or `React.useMemo` in two renders                   | No counterpart. `setup` runs one time, so a function or an object that it makes keeps its identity. Give the case a `not-applicable` row.                                                                                                                 |
| A case that changes a plain variable that an accessor of `createItems` reads, then renders the part again  | The accessor reads a `shallowRef`, and the case writes the ref. The root calls the accessor in a `computed`. A change of a plain variable does not make it call the accessor again, and a change of a ref that the accessor read does.                    |
| A commit count from `React.Profiler`                                                                       | A count of the calls of a `MutationObserver` that observes `container` with `childList` and `subtree`. For a popup in a portal, it observes `document.body`.                                                                                              |
| `store.subscribe(listener)` that records each notification of the store                                    | A `watch` on a getter of `store.state`, with the default flush. The watcher does not run between two writes of one function. The case thus sees a split of the writes over two watchers, and not a split into two `update` calls of one function.         |
| `ReactDOM.createPortal(children, container)`                                                               | `<Teleport to={container}>`                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| A case that presses a node in a React portal, or sends it a key, to show that the part ignores it          | No counterpart. A Vue event bubbles through the DOM and not through the component tree, so a `Teleport` gives the part no such press or key. Give the case a `not-applicable` row.                                                                        |
| `render(node, { container })`, with a container in a shadow root                                           | `render(<Teleport to={container}>{node}</Teleport>)`. `render` has no `container` option.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `ReactDOM.flushSync` in a listener of a test, to unmount a part                                            | `unmount()` of the `render` result, which unmounts synchronously                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `ReactDOM.flushSync` in a listener of a shadow root, so that `element.click()` acts as trusted input       | A trusted click: focus the button, then `await userEvent.keyboard('{Enter}')` with `userEvent` of `vitest/browser`. The browser runs a microtask checkpoint after each listener of a trusted event, so Vue applies the update before the next listener.   |
| `await flushMicrotasks()` before an event that must come after a timer of 0 ms of the part                 | `await wait(0)`. `flushMicrotasks` resolves in a message task, which can come before the timer.                                                                                                                                                           |
| The unmount of one part in a listener, where the other parts stay                                          | A second `render` with a provider of the root context, and `unmount()` of its result. See `ScrollAreaThumb.test.tsx`, "handles the scrollbar unmounting…".                                                                                                |
| `<React.Activity>` with `mode`                                                                             | `<KeepAlive>` around a child that the test renders only while it is visible. The title of the block stays.                                                                                                                                                |
| `<React.StrictMode>` around the tree                                                                       | No wrapper. Vue has no Strict Mode.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `createRenderer({ strict })` and `render(element, { strict })`                                             | No option. Vue has no Strict Mode. The case runs one time.                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `describe.each([false, true])('… (strict: %s)', …)`                                                        | `describe` with the same title. The block runs one time.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `it.each([false, true])('… (strict: %s)', …)`                                                              | `it` with the same title. The case runs one time.                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| A row of `it.each` that runs the case in Strict Mode                                                       | The row with no Strict Mode. Its label loses the words about Strict Mode.                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A row of `it.each` that mocks `useId` to give `undefined`, as in React 17                                  | No counterpart. Remove the row of `it.each`. The case keeps its title, so it needs no exception row. `useVaseUiId` gives an id in `setup`.                                                                                                                |
| `React.lazy(loader)`                                                                                       | `defineAsyncComponent(loader)`, then `await flushMicrotasks()` before the assertions                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `act(fn)`                                                                                                  | `fn()`, then `await flushMicrotasks()`. Where `fn` makes the part start a 0 ms timer, see [Test rules](#test-rules).                                                                                                                                      |
| `ignoreActWarnings()`                                                                                      | No call. Vue gives no warning about `act`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `React.startTransition(() => setX(value))`                                                                 | `void nextTick(() => { x.value = value; })`. Vue has no low-priority update. The write comes in the tick after the event.                                                                                                                                 |
| A write in `React.startTransition` that renders a component that suspends, and a later write that drops it | A `<Suspense>` whose default slot gets a component with an `async setup` that awaits a promise that never resolves. A resolved `<Suspense>` keeps its content while a new branch is pending, as a transition does. The title stays.                       |
| A key press directly after an open, in a case that upstream runs only in jsdom                             | First wait for the focus of the item, as [Test rules](#test-rules) says for a focus in an animation frame. In a browser, the focus moves into the popup one animation frame after the open.                                                               |
| `act(() => trigger.focus())` on the trigger of a closed Select, before a key                               | Focus the trigger, then wait with `waitFor` until the items exist. The trigger mounts the items in a 0 ms timer. `flushMicrotasks` resolves in the next task, and a browser can run that task before the task of the timer.                               |
| A read of a part right after `render`, where the part mounts after the first measurement                   | `await flushMicrotasks()` after `render`, then the read. The `render` of Base UI runs in `act`, which runs the microtask of the measurement.                                                                                                              |
| `toBeVisible()` on a part of an open popup that has no trigger                                             | Add a trigger. The positioner has no anchor, so it has no first position and keeps `opacity: 0`.                                                                                                                                                          |
| `clock.withFakeTimers()` of `createRenderer()`                                                             | `vi.useFakeTimers()` in `beforeEach`, and `vi.useRealTimers()` in `afterEach`                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `clock.tick(n)`                                                                                            | `vi.advanceTimersByTime(n)`, then `await flushMicrotasks()`                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `clock.tickAsync(n)`                                                                                       | `await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(n)`, then `await flushMicrotasks()`                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `clockOptions: { shouldAdvanceTime: true }` of `createRenderer()`                                          | `vi.useFakeTimers({ shouldAdvanceTime: true })`. A file that asserts a time limit gives no option, and a case of that file that calls `user` calls `vi.useRealTimers()` first. See [Test helpers](#test-helpers).                                         |
| `advanceReactClock(clock, n)`                                                                              | `await flushMicrotasks()`, `await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(n)`, then `await flushMicrotasks()`. The async form runs the flushes of Vue between two timers.                                                                                             |
| `renderHook(() => useX())`                                                                                 | A component in the test that calls the composable in `setup` and gives the result to the test                                                                                                                                                             |
| `toasts` of `Toast.useToastManager()`, such as `toasts.map(…)` or `toasts[0]`                              | `toasts.value`, because the composable returns a ref                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| A React element as the label of a toast, such as `title: <strong>Saved</strong>`                           | A function that returns the vnode: `title: () => h('strong', 'Saved')`. A toast keeps no vnode.                                                                                                                                                           |
| A React element in a row of `it.each` or `describe.each`, or in a constant that a case renders             | A function that returns the vnode. The case calls it. See [Test rules](#test-rules).                                                                                                                                                                      |
| The label of the toast as the children of its part, such as `<Toast.Title>{toast.title}</Toast.Title>`     | `<Toast.Title />`. The part shows the label of its toast.
