A default slot counts as content also when it renders `undefined`.                                                                                                                              |
| Children of a label part that are `undefined` in one state, such as `{explicit ? 'Title' : undefined}`     | `v-slots={explicit ? { default: () => 'Title' } : {}}`. The part then has a default slot only while it has content.                                                                                                                                       |
| A function component that calls a hook and spreads the rest of its props                                   | `defineComponent` with a setup function that returns the render function. Declare the props that the hook reads. Set `inheritAttrs: false` and spread `attrs`.                                                                                            |
| A function component of a test with a prop that starts with `on`, such as `onRenameClick`                  | `defineComponent` with a setup function and `props: ['onRenameClick']`. Vue passes an undeclared `on…` prop to the root as a listener. It warns when that root renders a fragment, as a part with no element does.                                        |
| `useTestInteractions([useX(…), …])` in a test component                                                    | The same call in `setup`. In the render function, call the functions of the result that the upstream test component calls. A call of these functions in `setup` reads the props only one time.                                                            |
| `useTestInteractions([useX(…)])`, where `useX` returns listeners with no `reference`, such as `useFocus`   | Call `useX(…)` in `setup`, and spread the object that it returns on the trigger: `<button {...props} />`. The object has the listeners and no `reference` member.                                                                                         |
| `<CompositeList>`, `<CompositeRoot>` or `<CompositeItem>` in a test                                        | The fixture part of `packages/vue/test/fixtures`, imported with the upstream name                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `useFloating` of `test/floating-ui-tests`                                                                  | No change. Some ports import `test/fixtures/useFloating.ts`, which re-exports the helper. Import the helper by its path.                                                                                                                                  |
| `floatingStyles` and `middleware` of that `useFloating`                                                    | No counterpart. The helper returns no styles and takes no middleware. Remove the `style` binding and the option.                                                                                                                                          |
| A component of `test/floating-ui-tests`, such as `Grid`                                                    | A component in the test file that renders it. It keeps only what the cases read. The upstream file has a `not-applicable` row in the family of that test file.                                                                                            |
| `Navigation.tsx` of `test/floating-ui-tests`                                                               | The same path, because the family `focus` ports the file. Its style sheet is a set of style objects, because the package has no type for a CSS module.                                                                                                    |
| A direct call of `userEvent`, such as `userEvent.hover(element)`, where each call needs a new pointer      | Keep the direct call. It makes a new instance, so its pointer does not leave the element of the call before.                                                                                                                                              |
| `resetBrowserPointer()` of `#test-utils`, in a `beforeEach` hook or in `onTestFinished`                    | No counterpart. Remove the call. `test/setup.ts` moves the real pointer of the browser off the page after a test that put it there.                                                                                                                       |
| `isScrollLocked(doc)` of `#test-utils`                                                                     | `isScrollLocked(doc)` of `test/fixtures/scrollDocument.ts`. It reads the computed overflow of the element that scrolls the page, and the marker `data-vase-ui-scroll-locked`.                                                                             |
| `pointerEvents: 'none'` in the `style` of an element, where it only keeps the real pointer off the element | No such property. `test/setup.ts` moves the real pointer of the browser off the page after a test that put it there.                                                                                                                                      |
| `{Esc}` in a `keyboard` call of user-event                                                                 | `{Escape}`. user-event sends the key `Esc` for `{Esc}`. React changes that key to `Escape`. Vue gives the event of the browser, and the parts compare the key with `Escape`.                                                                              |
| A `beforeEach` hook at the top of a file that also has cases of Vase UI's own                              | The same hook in each `describe` block of the Base UI cases. A hook at the top runs for each case of the file.                                                                                                                                            |
| A `beforeEach` hook that gives `HTMLElement.prototype` the property `inert`                                | No counterpart. jsdom has no `inert`, and a browser has it.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| A read directly after a `fireEvent` call, before the effects of the part run                               | Keep the promise of `fireEvent`, make the read, then `await` the promise. An `await` before the read lets the effects run first.                                                                                                                          |
| `renderToString` of the renderer, with no `hydrate()`                                                      | A case in the `.node.test.tsx` file of the same folder, with `renderToString` of `vue/server-renderer`. Give the upstream case a `replaced` row.                                                                                                          |
| `renderToString` of the renderer, then `hydrate()`                                                         | The case stays in the ported test file with the same title, so it needs no row. Use `renderOnServer` and `hydrate`, which need a browser. A case that acts on the server HTML before `hydrate()` calls `hydrateAfter`. See [Test helpers](#test-helpers). |
| A `React.Suspense` around a part, so that the part hydrates after its root                                 | A fixture SFC with `defineAsyncComponent(() => Promise.resolve(Part))` inside `<Suspense>`. Hydrate it by hand. See [Test helpers](#test-helpers).                                                                                                        |
| `expect(fn).toErrorDev(message)` or `.toWarnDev(message)`                                                  | A spy that has `mockImplementation`, on `console.error` or `console.warn`. Call `fn`. Assert that the spy got that one call: `toHaveBeenCalledExactlyOnceWith(message)`. The message starts with `Vase UI:`.                                              |
| `toErrorDev` for a warning that React DOM gives, such as the warning for a `NaN` value                     | No counterpart, because Vue gives no such warning. The case runs with no wrapper and keeps its other assertions. State the removal in the body of the pull request.                                                                                       |
| A `console.error` spy around a `render` that rejects, and no assertion on the spy                          | No spy. React writes the error of a component to the console, and Vue does not. Assert the Vue warning in the list of `takeReportedMessages()`. See [Test rules](#test-rules).                                                                            |
| An assertion that no call of a console spy holds a text: `mock.calls.some(...)` is `false`                 | `expect(takeReportedMessages()).toEqual([])` at that step, with no spy. It asserts that no message of any text came until then.                                                                                                                           |
| A count of the calls of a console spy that hold a text: `mock.calls.filter(...)`                           | Compare `spy.mock.calls` with the whole list: `expect(spy.mock.calls).toEqual([[first], [second]])`.                                                                                                                                                      |
| `childNodes`, `firstChild` or `nextSibling` on the element of a part                                       | `children` or `querySelector`                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| A number as a length in a style object, such as `top: 1`                                                   | A string with its unit, such as `top: '1px'`                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `<style dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: style }} />`                                                    | `<style>{style}</style>`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `toHaveComputedStyle(obj)`                                                                                 | `toHaveStyle(obj)`, which also reads the computed style                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `toHaveComputedStyle({})`                                                                                  | `not.toHaveAttribute('style')`, because `toHaveStyle({})` fails for an empty object                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `expect(element).toBeInaccessible()`                                                                       | `expect(isInaccessible(element)).toBe(true)`. `#test-utils` exports `isInaccessible` of `@testing-library/dom`, which gives the same answer.                                                                                                              |
| `toBeEmptyDOMElement` on a part with an empty default slot, or on the old target of a `Teleport`           | `expect(element.innerHTML).toBe('')`, because the slot leaves empty text nodes. Vue 3.5.12 leaves an empty text node in the old target of a `Teleport`.                                                                                                   |
| An event                                                                                                   | Add `await`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `expect(() => fireEvent.x()).not.toThrow()`                                                                | `await fireEvent.x()`. A listener that throws fails the test.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `fireEvent.click(element, { button: 2 })`                                                                  | `await user.pointer({ keys: '[MouseRight]', target: element })`, a real press of the right button. React drops that click, and a browser sends none.                                                                                                      |
| A drag release: `fireEvent.mouseDown(trigger)`, then `fireEvent.mouseUp(item)`                             | `fireEvent.mouseDown(trigger, clientCenter(trigger))`, then the same `mouseUp` call. A dispatched event with no point has the point (0, 0). An item ignores a release at the point of the press (`VUE-84`).                                               |
| `contextMenu` at (20, 20), then `pointerMove` and a right `mouseUp` over an item at (24, 24)               | The same calls, with `pointerMove` and `mouseUp` at (30, 30): 10 px from the open point on each axis. An item ignores a release within 8 px of the point where the context menu opened (`VUE-84`).                                                        |
| An enter or leave call of `fireEvent` on a descendant of the element that has the listener                 | The same call on the element that has the listener. The calls are `pointerEnter`, `pointerLeave`, `mouseEnter` and `mouseLeave`. The native events do not bubble. React makes its events from events that bubble, such as `pointerover`.                  |
| `fireEvent.mouseOut(a, { relatedTarget: b })`, where a leave listener of `a` must run                      | The same call, then `fireEvent.mouseLeave(a, { relatedTarget: b })`. React makes the leave event of `a` from `mouseout`. A browser also sends `mouseleave`.                                                                                               |
| A `pointerout` event that a case builds and dispatches to run a leave listener                             | A `pointerleave` event on the element that has the listener. React makes `onPointerLeave` from `pointerout`. The native `pointerleave` does not bubble.                                                                                                   |
| `fireEvent.change(input, { target: { value } })` on a text input                                           | `fireEvent.input(input, { target: { value } })`. It is the event of real typing.                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `fireEvent.focus(element)` and `fireEvent.blur(element)` on a part that has a focus handler                | `fireEvent.focusIn(element)` and `fireEvent.focusOut(element)`. The part listens to `focusin` and `focusout`, and a dispatched `focus` event is not one of them.                                                                                          |
| `event.preventDefault()` in an `onFocus` or `onBlur` handler of a consumer                                 | `event.preventVaseUIHandler()` in the `onFocusin` or `onFocusout` handler. See [DOM event names](#dom-event-names).                                                                                                                                       |
| `fireEvent.touchStart`, `touchMove` or `touchEnd` with a plain object in `touches`. A browser refuses it.  | `fireTouch.start`, `move` or `end` of `#test-utils` with the same lists. See [Test helpers](#test-helpers).                                                                                                                                               |
| A `setPointerCapture` call that must capture the pointer                                                   | A real pointer: `userEvent` of `vitest/browser`. `ScrollAreaScrollbar.test.tsx`, "track click by axis" is the model.                                                                                                                                      |
| `waitFor` on a state that a timer of the part ends, directly after an event                                | A direct assertion after the awaited event. `fireEvent` resolves after the Vue flush, in the same task, so the timer cannot end the state first.                                                                                                          |
| `waitFor(callback, { timeout })` or `waitFor(callback, { interval })`                                      | `waitFor(callback)`. The limit is 180 frames of the page.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `waitFor` with a `queryBy` query that must give `null`                                                     | A direct assertion where the absence is true when the awaited action resolves. Otherwise a wait on an element that the case got before the action, and then the direct assertion. See [Test rules](#test-rules).                                          |
| `waitFor` with a `queryBy` query that must give an element                                                 | The same wait with the `getBy` query of that name. The `getBy` query throws while the element is absent.                                                                                                                                                  |
| A `findBy` query with options for its wait, or `vi.waitFor(callback)`                                      | The `findBy` query with no such options, or `waitFor(callback)` of `#test-utils`.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| An engine condition (`isWebKit`, `platform.engine.gecko`, a Blink check)                                   | No change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A case with no engine condition that fails in one engine, because the source has a branch for that engine  | No condition. In that engine, the case asserts what the branch does, in place of the Base UI assertion. It reads the same flag of `platform` as the source. The CI of Base UI runs its browser tests only in Chromium.                                    |
| A `beforeEach` hook that skips each case by the user agent of Safari                                       | Remove the hook when each case passes in WebKit. State the removal in the body of the pull request.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `isJSDOM`                                                                                                  | Always `false`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| A case under `skipIf(!isJSDOM)` that sets `process.env.NODE_ENV` to `'production'`                         | A case in the `.node.test` file of the same folder, with `vi.stubEnv('NODE_ENV', 'production')`. Give the upstream case a `replaced` row. Vitest sets the value when it transforms a file for a browser.                                                  |
| A function child of `Combobox.List`                                                                        | The slot `item`: `{{ item: ({ item }: { item: string }) => … }}`                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| An attribute on a part that renders no element, such as `data-testid` on `Combobox.Value`                  | Remove the attribute. Vue warns when a part with no element gets an attribute.                                                                                                                                                                            |
| A case that removes an attribute of a part with `{undefined}`                                              | A `replaced` row that names `VUE-103`. The case of the family gives `null` to remove the attribute, and it asserts that `undefined` keeps it.                                                                                                             |
| A mock of `SafeReact.captureOwnerStack`                                                                    | No counterpart. Remove the mock. Vue has no owner stack.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `user.hover` that must move the highlight of a list                                                        | `user.hover`, then `fireEvent.mouseMove(element, { movementX: 1 })`. Before a `pointerleave`, also `fireEvent.pointerMove(element, { pointerType: 'mouse', movementX: 1 })`. In WebKit, a list ignores a `mousemove` or a `pointermove` with no movement. |
| `fireEvent.mouseMove` with no movement that must move the highlight of a list                              | The same call with `movementX: 1`. In WebKit, a list ignores a `mousemove` with no movement.                                                                                                                                                              |
| A spy in a `render` element that counts the renders of a part                                              | A `.test.vue` fixture that calls the spy in the `#render` slot. In TSX, each render of a parent gives the part new slots, and Vue renders the part again.                                                                                                 |
| `reactMajor < 19` in a condition                                                                           | Always `false`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| An `eslint-disable` comment that ESLint reports                                                            | Remove it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

- TypeScript cannot infer the type of the parameter of a `v-slots` function.
- A render function of Base UI can return nothing, because the case only records the state. The slot of a Vue part must return an element: `<div {...props} />`. Without an element, the part gives a warning.
- Vue's JSX types declare no `Capture` attribute. Vue reads the suffix at run time.
- A helper component of a test can spread its props on a part. Give these props the type of the props of the instance, such as `InstanceType<typeof NumberField.Root>['$props']`. The type `NumberField.Root.Props` has no listener of an emit, such as `onValueChange`.
- A Vue handler gets the DOM event. It has no `nativeEvent`.
- React derives `which` of a keydown event from `keyCode`, and a Vue handler reads the native `which`. A constructed event gets `which` from `keyCode` in Chromium and not in Firefox and WebKit. A case that sets `keyCode` thus sets `which` to the same value.
- Vue binds a `ref` to the component whose render function makes the vnode. A vnode from the body of a test has no such component, so Vue drops its `ref`.
- Vue calls a slot in the render of the part that has the slot. A count that a slot reads thus renders only that part again. To render a root with no element of its own again, read the count in the render function of the test component.
- Vue has no ref forwarding. `resolveElement` reads the `element` that the `Wrapper` exposes.
- Vue sets a ref object after the render, and it calls a function ref during the patch. A part reads the exposed `element` during its own patch.
- `act` is a React function. `flushMicrotasks` is one of the test helpers.
- A function in `setup` reads the current props, so it needs no `useStableCallback`.
- A case that changes a prop between `0` and `-0` writes a `shallowRef` that the composable reads. Vue compares vnode props with `!==`.
- Vue has no `Profiler`. A `MutationObserver` gets one call for the DOM changes of one Vue flush.
- Vue puts empty text nodes around a fragment. `childNodes` has them. `children` does not.
- A case under `skipIf(isJSDOM)` runs.
- A case that upstream runs only in jsdom (`skipIf(!isJSDOM)`) is ported to the browser, or it gets an exception row.
- Such a case that clicks a link adds a `click` listener on `window` that calls `preventDefault()`. A browser otherwise loads the `href`, and Vitest then cannot run the test file.
- A case that upstream skips with a `skip()` call in its body runs when it passes in each engine. Remove the call, and state the removal in the body of the pull request.
- A ported case can pass while the behavior in its title is broken. Add assertions that fail when that behavior breaks. State them in the body of the pull request. When the source has a branch for one engine, an added assertion for that branch reads the same flag of `platform`.
- A case that fires its own `load` and `error` events on a real `<img>` holds the events of the browser. A capture listener on `document` stops each event that has `isTrusted`. See `holdBrowserImageEvents` in `avatar/image/AvatarImage.test.tsx`.
- ESLint reports an `eslint-disable` comment that names a rule of a plugin that this repository does not have. It also reports a comment that disables nothing.
- `#test-utils` is `packages/vue/test/index.ts`. It replaces the `#test-utils` and `@mui/internal-test-utils` of upstream.
- The counterpart of an upstream file of test components, such as `toast/utils/test-utils.tsx`, is a `.ts` file with the same name. It exports each component of the upstream file from an SFC with a `.test.vue` name. The lint refuses a `.tsx` file in `src` that is not a test. See [Test helpers](#test-helpers).
- `toErrorDev` and `toWarnDev` are custom matchers of upstream. For `.not.toErrorDev()`, assert that the spy got no call.
- `renderHook` is a function of `@testing-library/react`. A composable needs the `setup` of a component.
- Without `inheritAttrs: false`, Vue also puts the attributes on the root element of the test component. Each listener then runs two times.
- `createRenderer()` has no `renderToString`. A server render runs in Node with no DOM.
- The row for `renderToString` with no `hydrate()` has one exception. A case that reads the layout of the server HTML needs a browser. It stays in the ported test file with the same title, so it needs no row. Each other case of its `describe` block stays with it. Each case inserts the HTML of `renderOnServer` with a fragment of a range. See `server-side rendering` in `slider/thumb/SliderThumb.test.tsx`.
- A row for a `describe` block also covers each case of the block that has no counterpart. Give the block a `replaced` row only when each case of the block moves to the `.node.test.tsx` file. `useButton` does this for `server-side rendering`.
- Vue renders an async component after its loader resolves. `flushMicrotasks` waits for that render.
- A spy as a slot gets one object with the slot props. Assert the fields of the first argument of the last call: `spy.mock.lastCall?.[0].value`.
- A module namespace is read-only. A test that must change a flag of `platform` mocks the module, as `utils/popups/event.test.ts` does.
- Vue calls a function ref again on each patch. A case that asserts the calls of a ref gives the ref to `useRenderElement`, which calls it only when the element changes.
- Where Base UI polls a state that exists only while an animation runs, a ported case records that state as [Test rules](#test-rules) says. It can also set `VASE_UI_ANIMATIONS_DISABLED` to `false`. The titles stay.
- Where Base UI waits with `waitFor` for a flag that an `animationend` or `transitionend` listener sets, the ported case waits for the event with `nextEvent`. It gives the action and the start event (`after`), as [Test rules](#test-rules) says. For an element that mounts with the action, the case listens on an ancestor that exists before the action, such as the `container` of `render`. Make sure that no other element in that ancestor runs a transition or an animation in the same frames.
- Where Base UI waits with `waitFor` for the removal of an element after its exit, the ported case gets the element before the action and waits with `waitFor`, as [Test rules](#test-rules) says. A case that asserts when the part removes the element gets the exit animations from `nextAnimationsRead`. It waits for the `finished` promise of each animation, and then for `flushMicrotasks()`. Then it asserts the removal directly. The part removes the element in the microtasks after the end of the animation.
- Vue makes each element with the `document` of the page, also in an app that it mounts in the document of a frame. Such an element has the classes of the window of the page, and the DOM matchers refuse it. Assert `isConnected` in place of `toBeInTheDocument()`.
- Where the Base UI cases of a file declare the same test component, the ported file can declare that component one time. A prop of the component gives a difference between the cases.
- Vitest fakes `requestAnimationFrame` with the other timers. With fake timers and `VASE_UI_ANIMATIONS_DISABLED` set to `false`, a closed part stays in its exit until the test moves the clock. A ported jsdom case that asserts a part in its exit thus sets the flag to `false`. With `true`, the part leaves at once.
- Where Base UI uses a short `waitFor` timeout to show that an open delay runs or does not run, a ported case asserts the open directly after the awaited event when the part opens in the task of that event. Otherwise the case records the time of the enter event and the time of the open in listeners. It then compares the difference with half of the delay. A slow run then cannot change the result.
- A Base UI case can start the timeout of a toast and then move the real pointer to the toast. On a busy machine, the command of the browser can take longer than the timeout, and the toast closes before the pointer comes. The ported case adds the toast with `timeout: 0` and moves the pointer to it. It then gives the toast its timeout with `manager.update`, and the timer starts paused because the pointer rests on the toast. Fake timers do not prevent this fault. While a command of `vitest/browser` waits for an element, as `click` and `hover` do, Vitest moves the fake clock by 50 ms every 50 ms. See `real browser input` in `toast/viewport/ToastViewport.test.tsx`.
- One `clock.tick(n)` of Base UI can run two timers. The second timer can read a state that Vue writes in the flush after the first timer. The ported case then calls `vi.useFakeTimers()` with no `shouldAdvanceTime`, and it moves the clock with `await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(n)`. The async form runs the flush of Vue between the two timers, as a browser does. A clock that moves by itself can run both timers in one step. The case `closes the parent menu after a nested submenu closes on delayed hover-out` of `navigation-menu/root/NavigationMenuRoot.test.tsx` is the example.
- A case that moves the focus out of a popup with `fireEvent` dispatches all its focus events in one task: `await Promise.all([fireEvent.focusOut(…), fireEvent.focusIn(…), …])`. The Base UI case calls `fireEvent` with no `await`, so its events come in one task.
- The focus manager checks each `focusout` event in a microtask. A `focusout` event in the popup sets `insideComponentTree`, and the next check clears it. With all events in one task, the check of the first `focusout` clears the marker, and the check of the last one closes the popup. With an `await` between the events, the check of the last one clears the marker, and the popup stays open. `validates when the popup is blurred` of `Select.Root` is the model.
- A popup that is open at the mount takes the focus one animation frame later. A focus that a case gives to the trigger before that frame does not stay. The case first waits for the focus in the popup, as [Test rules](#test-rules) says for a focus in an animation frame.
- A Base UI case can press the trigger of a `Menu`, a `Select` or a `Combobox` while the exit of an earlier close runs. The popup is in the document during the exit, so a `findBy` query does not wait for the open. The ported case waits with `waitFor` for `aria-expanded="true"` on the trigger or the input before its next step.
- With `vi.useFakeTimers({ shouldAdvanceTime: true })`, the frame of a press of the trigger of a `Menu`, a `Select` or a `Combobox` runs before `await user.click(trigger)` resolves. Vitest moves that clock by 20 ms each 20 ms of real time. One step runs the timer that `user` waits for after the press. The same step runs the frame of the press, which is due 16 ms or less after the `mousedown`. The next `user` step waits for one more step of the clock, so its events come in a later millisecond of the mocked `Date.now()` than the open. A ported case under that clock can thus keep the read or the `user` step of Base UI directly after the press. The table row for such a step is for a case with a real clock.
- `Select.Trigger` mounts the popup element and its items in a 0 ms timer after it gets the focus. `Combobox.Trigger` mounts them after the focus and at `mousedown`, but not when `Combobox.Root` has the `items` prop. The popup element then stays in the document while the popup is closed, and its positioner has the `hidden` attribute. A `findByText` or `findByTestId` query thus finds an item of the closed popup, and it does not wait for the open. A `findByRole` query does not find an element inside a `hidden` element, so it waits for the open.
- A menu focuses a hovered submenu trigger in an animation frame. In a menubar, a case that hovers a submenu trigger and then moves the pointer into the submenu waits for that focus before the move. Wait as [Test rules](#test-rules) says for a focus in an animation frame. Until that frame, the menubar trigger can keep the focus. A pointer in the submenu then moves the focus from that trigger to a submenu item, and `useFocus` of the trigger closes the menu.
- `addEventListener` of `src/common` removes a listener with the `options` argument that it added it with, also when that argument is `undefined`. A case that asserts a call of `removeEventListener` with `toHaveBeenCalledWith(type, listener)` thus fails. The case asserts that one call has that type and that listener.

### Test helpers

This section is the contract of the test helpers. Each helper must obey it. The helpers keep upstream's names.

- `createRenderer()` returns `{ render }`.
- `render` takes a vnode or a function that returns a vnode. It resolves to the queries, `container`, `unmount`, `user`, `rerender` and `setProps`.
- `render` mounts the vnode in the app of `createTestApp`. It is a Vue app, or a Vapor app in the project `vapor`. The helpers do not use `@testing-library/vue`, because it depends on another version of `@testing-library/dom`.
- The app of `render` records each Vue warning for the check of the test setup. Its `warnHandler` does not throw. After a `warnHandler` throws, Vue gives no more warnings on that page.
- `user` is `userEvent.setup({ document })` of `@testing-library/user-event`, at the version that upstream pins.
- `user` waits for a timer after each action, so it does not return under a fake clock that does not move. A fake clock that moves with real time can end a timer of a part between two awaited steps. A browser can run the 0 ms timer of `user` in less than 1 ms. Thus a call of `user` does not wait for a later millisecond.
- After a mount, a `rerender` or a `setProps`, the helper waits until `Date.now()` is later than the latest stamp that Vue can give a listener of the change. Vue stamps a listener with `Date.now()` when it attaches it. Vue skips the second and later listeners of an event that occurs in the millisecond when Vue attached them.
- The system can set the wall clock back by some milliseconds while a test runs. `Date.now()` is then smaller than the stamp of a listener that Vue attached before the step. Thus the helper reads `Date.now()` and `performance.now()` before the change. `performance.now()` has no steps, so the time that it measures, added to the first `Date.now()`, gives the latest stamp. After a step forward, `Date.now()` itself is the latest stamp.
- On macOS, one read of `Date.now()` can be late. It gives a time that is some milliseconds later than the read before it and the read after it. Thus the helper ends its wait only when two reads of `Date.now()`, one directly after the other, are later than the latest stamp.
- No wait covers a step back between the end of the wait and the event. In Firefox and WebKit, `performance.now()` gives whole milliseconds, so the wait can end too early after a step back during the change. No wait covers such a late read when Vue stamps a listener.
- When `vi.getMockedSystemTime()` gives a time, the helper first moves that clock by one or more whole frames of 16 ms with `vi.setSystemTime`. A mocked clock does not move while the helper waits. Fake timers and a call of `vi.setSystemTime` with no fake timers both mock the clock.
- A mocked `Date` with a real `performance.now()` gives a latest stamp 16 ms or more after `Date.now()` when the change takes more than 15 ms. The move is then the smallest number of whole frames that makes `Date.now()` later than the stamp.
- `vi.setSystemTime` runs no timer. It moves each pending fake timer by the same time, so each timer keeps its delay.
- The fake clock of Vitest runs a frame each 16 ms from the time when fake timers start. The move is whole frames, so each frame callback stays in its frame. A frame callback that the mount requests runs at the next `vi.advanceTimersToNextFrame()`.
- Each move makes a mocked `Date.now()` at least 16 ms later. A part that measures a time with `Date.now()`, such as the store of Toast, thus measures at least 16 ms more than its timers waited.
- Fake timers with a `toFake` list that has no `Date` keep the real `Date.now()`. The helper then also waits until the real `Date.now()` is later than the latest stamp.
- `waitForLaterMillisecond(action)` runs `action`, then waits as `render` does after a mount. `#test-utils` exports it. It resolves to the result of `action`. Give it the action that mounts the target of the next event, with the waits for that mount: `await waitForLaterMillisecond(() => user.click(trigger))`. The helper reads the clocks before the action, because a step back during the action cannot be seen after it. Without the wait, Vue can skip the listener of the target, because the event comes in the millisecond when Vue attached that listener.
- `fireEvent` is a function of the test helpers. It dispatches the event, waits for `nextTick()` and returns the result of the dispatch. Upstream tests read that result, which is `false` when a listener prevents the default action.
- `fireTouch.start`, `move`, `end` and `cancel` dispatch a touch event with `touches`, `targetTouches` and `changedTouches` through `fireEvent`. A point needs only `clientX` and `clientY`. The event is a `TouchEvent` where the browser can construct one. WebKit on a desktop cannot construct a `Touch`. There the event is an `Event` with the three lists.
- `flushMicrotasks` resolves in the next task. Each flush that Vue queued is then complete.
- `waitFor(callback)` calls `callback` until it does not throw, and it resolves with the result. It calls `callback` immediately, after each change of the DOM of the page, and in each frame.
- `waitFor` rejects with the last error of `callback` when the page rendered 180 frames and no call passed. The error of a failed query already has the DOM of its container in its message. `waitFor` adds the DOM of the page to the message of each other `Error`.
- In each call of `callback` but the last, a failed `getByRole` query gives no role report. The wait of Testing Library gives no role report in any call. The report reads the role and the style of each element of the container. The last call gives the full error.
- `waitFor` takes no options. It replaces `waitFor` of `@testing-library/dom`, whose limit is 1 s.
- `waitFor` rejects with a `TypeError` when `callback` returns a promise. It does not wait for a promise, and it does not call `callback` again after a rejection. Await the action first. Then give `waitFor` a synchronous `callback`.
- The `findBy` queries of `screen`, of `within` and of the result of `render` wait with `waitFor`. A `findBy` query throws when a call gives it options for its wait.
- The error of a failed `findBy` query has the stack of the call, so Vitest shows the line of the test. The query runs in a frame callback, and the stack of that callback has no line of the test. `vi.defineHelper` gives the error the stack of the call.
- `waitFor` counts the frames of the browser, also under fake timers. Fake timers replace `requestAnimationFrame`, and a fake frame comes only when the test moves the clock.
- A helper file that `test/index.ts` loads imports `waitFor` from `waitFor.ts`, and `screen` and `within` from `queries.ts`, by a relative path, because `#test-utils` is that index. A test file imports them from `#test-utils`.
- Only `queries.ts` imports `screen`, `within` and `queries` of `@testing-library/dom`.
- After each test in a browser, `test/setup.ts` waits for one 0 ms timer of the browser. The wait comes after each unmount and before the check of the messages. The next test then starts in a new task. A 0 ms timer that the last test set in the task in which the test ended ran before the new task. The browser delays a timer by 4 ms or more when timers nest more than five deep. A delayed timer that the last test set in an earlier task can run in the next test. Without the wait, a test that ends in a `ResizeObserver` callback gives the next test the loop error of the browser. Fake timers do not replace this timer.
- After each test in which the page got a trusted `pointerover` event, `test/setup.ts` moves the real pointer of the browser to a point outside the page. The move comes before the wait for the timer. A press in a frame of the page also gives the page that event, on the frame element. Without the move, the pointer stays where the test left it. An element that a later test renders under the pointer then gets hover events from the browser. Chromium and Firefox send them in two frames or fewer, and WebKit sends them later.
- After each test in a browser, `test/setup.ts` waits for the end of each command of `userEvent` of `vitest/browser` that the test started. This wait comes before the pointer move, because a command can put the pointer in the page. A test that times out in a command ends while the browser still does the command. Without the wait, the keys and the pointer moves of the command reach the next test. A key that the command holds, such as `{Shift>}`, also stays down in the next test. Before each test, Vitest releases only the keys of the commands that ended.
- The function of a test that times out in a command of `userEvent` continues when the command ends. When the command ends after the `onTestFinished` hooks that the test registered, the call of the command rejects. The next steps of the function thus do not run in the next test. Only its `catch` and `finally` blocks run. A command that the function starts after a wait of another kind, such as a frame, can still run in the next test.
- In a browser, `test/setup.ts` replaces `requestPointerLock` with a function that rejects, as a browser that refuses the lock does. Whether a browser gives a pointer lock to the page of a test depends on the engine and on the platform. With a lock, Firefox puts a second pointer in the page. The move of the setup does not take that pointer off the page, and the page keeps it for each later test file. An element that a later test renders under that pointer gets hover events from the browser. A test that needs a pointer lock mocks `requestPointerLock`.
- `holdPress(element)` presses the main button of the real pointer at the center of `element` and holds it. It resolves to `{ point, release }`. `release()` lets the button go at `point`. When the case does not call `release()`, the helper lets the button go when the case ends.
- `clientCenter(element)` returns the client point of the center of `element` as `{ clientX, clientY }`, for the init of a dispatched mouse event.
- `touchDrag(from, to, steps)` and `penDrag(from, to, steps)` drag a real touch and a real pen from one client point of the test page to another, in `steps` moves, and lift it at the last point. `penDrag` then moves the pen out of the page. They send the events through the protocol of Chromium, so a case that calls them is only for Chromium.
- `describeConformance` has no hydration case. A server render needs the path of a module. The suite gets a vnode. The test of a part calls the hydration helpers.
- In the project `vapor`, `describeConformance` adds the cases `Vapor consumer`. They render the part from `test/conformanceTests/VaporConsumer.vue`, a component in Vapor mode, with `as`, with `#render` and `:ref="props.ref"`, and with `#render` and no `:ref`. The consumer gives the part the default slot of the vnode of the call.
- The consumer renders a static element for each tag of `testRenderPropWith`. Add a tag that a new call needs.
- `describeConformance` needs a part that has its element when the `render` option of the call resolves. For a part with no element at mount, that option makes the element appear: it renders an open root, or it does the action that mounts the part. The call for `Avatar.Image` needs no action, because the test file mocks `Image` and the element appears in the flush after the mount.
- The call for `ScrollArea.Corner` waits for the first measurement of the viewport, because the corner has no element before it. Its `render` option calls `await flushMicrotasks()`.
- The call for a portal part, and for each part in it, such as `Tooltip.Popup`, also calls `await flushMicrotasks()` in its `render` option. The portal renders its element and its content in the flush after the mount.
- `waitForPositioned(positioner)` waits until the positioner passes `toBeVisible()`. Before its first position, a positioner has `opacity: 0`.
- `popupConformanceTests`, `positionerConformanceTests`, `detachedTriggersConformanceTests` and `viewportConformanceTests` are the shared suites of a popup family. `createComponent` of each suite except `detachedTriggersConformanceTests` gets the props of the parts and returns a vnode.
- `popupListConformanceTests` is the shared suite of a popup that moves a highlight through a list of items, such as Menu, Select and Combobox. `createComponent` gets `root`, `items`, `disabledItems`, `onItemClick`, `scrollerStyle` and `itemStyle`, and it returns a vnode. In that vnode, give `data-testid="trigger"` to the trigger, `data-testid="popup"` to the popup and `data-testid="scroller"` to the element that scrolls the items.
- `root.ref` of `popupConformanceTests` is a function ref that gets the exposed actions of the root. A case that sets it renders a function.
- `popupConformanceTests` gives a hover trigger `delay: 0`, so the popup opens at the `mouseenter` event. A `delay` in the `trigger` props of a case replaces it.
- `createComponent` of `positionerConformanceTests` gives `render` of the positioner props to the `#render` slot of the part. Under that slot, the part renders no popup.
- `detachedTriggersConformanceTests` gets the parts of the family as components. It puts `triggerProps` on each trigger. The suite gives each click that opens the popup to `waitForLaterMillisecond(action)` as its action. The suite dispatches the hover events. Do not give the triggers `pointer-events: none`, because `test/setup.ts` moves the real pointer off the page after a test that put it there. Set `outsideElementsInert` for a popup that sets `inert` outside it: the suite then expects the focus on the active trigger after a close.
- `viewportConformanceTests` gets `render`, `createComponent` and `openOn`. `createComponent` gets the props of the parts and `renderContent(payload)`, and it puts that content in the Viewport part. `openOn` is `'click'` or `'focus'`: the event that opens the popup or moves it to another trigger.
- `dialogRootSharedTests({ parts, popupRole, render })` is the shared suite of the roots of Dialog and Alert Dialog. `parts` is the namespace of the parts of the family, and `popupRole` is the role of its popup.
- `nestedPopupCloseTests({ parts, wrapPopup, render })` is the shared suite of Dialog and Drawer for a popup in the popup that closes in the same update (`BUG-5519`). `parts` is the namespace of the parts of the family. `wrapPopup` puts a popup in the parts that the family needs around it, such as `Drawer.Viewport`.
- `createFormDataSpy(name)` returns a `vi.fn` for a `submit` listener. The spy calls `preventDefault()` on the event. It returns the entry of the form for `name`, or `null` when the form has no such entry. Read the last result with `spy.mock.results.at(-1)?.value`.
- `pasteText(target, value)` dispatches a `paste` event whose `clipboardData` gives `value` as `text/plain`, and it resolves as `fireEvent` does. Chromium and WebKit refuse a plain object as the `clipboardData` of `fireEvent.paste`, so the helper makes the event itself.
- `hiddenInputSizeTests({ render, createComponent })` is a shared suite for a part with a hidden input that has no `name`. `createComponent` gets `{ required }` and returns a vnode. The suite makes the input invalid and asserts that it has zero size. After `reportValidity()` and `await flushMicrotasks()`, it asserts that the input is 1px by 1px.
- `hiddenInputTypeTests({ render, createComponent })` is a shared suite for a part with a hidden input that `getHiddenInputType` types. `createComponent` gets `{ name, autoComplete, required }` and returns a vnode. The suite asserts that the input is `type="hidden"` only while the part has none of them and no field.
- `heldMountFormResetTests({ render, renderControl, change, readState, defaultState, changedState })` is a shared case for a part that calls `useFormReset`. `renderControl` returns the uncontrolled control with a `name` and a default value. The case renders `HeldBranch` of `createHeldBranch` in a form, and it calls `mountWhileHeld` and `show`. It asserts that `readState()` gives `defaultState`, awaits `change()`, and asserts `changedState`. Then it resets the form and asserts `defaultState` again. `readState` reads a state that the part shows, such as `aria-checked`. The browser resets a native input itself, so the value of that input can be the default value also when the part did not follow the reset. The test file of each family whose part calls `useFormReset` calls the case. `RadioGroup.test.tsx` calls it for `Radio.Root`, which follows a reset only in a group.
- `heldMountListTests({ render, renderList, check })` is the shared case of a part that owns a composite list. `renderList(withLastItem)` returns that part and its items, and it renders the last item only when `withLastItem` is `true`. The case mounts the part while `KeepAlive` holds its branch. It sets `withLastItem` in a later flush and then shows the branch. `check(user)` asserts a result that needs each item. It can first do a step of a user, such as a press of an arrow key. The test file of each part that calls `useCompositeList` or `useCompositeRoot` calls it.
- `removedPartHydrationTests({ file, component, cases })` is the shared suite of a part that binds an `aria-*` value or a state attribute that a later part gives. `file` is the path of the fixture for `renderOnServer`, and `component` is the fixture for `hydrate`. The fixture removes or changes the later part in the first flush after the mount. Each case asserts `serverValue`, the value of `attribute` on the element of `selector` in the server HTML. Then it hydrates the fixture and asserts `clientValue`, the value after the hydration. `clientValue` is `null` for no attribute. For an id that Vue generates, `clientValue` is `{ idOf: selector }`, and the case reads the id of that element.
- `popupFocusPropsTests({ render, createComponent })` is the shared suite for `initialFocus` and `finalFocus` of a popup. `createComponent` gets `{ initialFocus, finalFocus, children }` and returns the popup with a trigger "Open" and a control "Close". Set `outsideElementsInert` for a popup that sets `inert` outside it. The suite then does not expect the trigger to keep the focus.
- `popupAnatomyTests({ render, createComponent, createPopup, createBackdropComponent, createBackdropAndPositionerComponent, createPendingPositionerComponent, reportsOpen })` is the shared suite of a popup root with no popup part (`VUE-72`). `createComponent` gets `{ open, onOpenChangeComplete, content }` and returns the root. Give the part that shows `data-testid="shown"` and the class `shown`. It is the positioner, or the backdrop of a dialog, an alert dialog or a drawer. Put `content` where the popup part goes.
- `createBackdropComponent` of `popupAnatomyTests` returns the root with a backdrop part and no positioner. Give the backdrop the same test id and class. The suite then runs its first two cases on that root too.
- `createBackdropAndPositionerComponent` of `popupAnatomyTests` returns the root with a backdrop part and a positioner with no popup part. Give the positioner the test id and the class, and give the backdrop neither. The suite then asserts that the close waits for the exit animation of the positioner. `PopoverRoot.test.tsx` gives it for the default element, and `NavigationMenuRoot.test.tsx` for the element function of `NavigationMenu.Root`.
- `createPendingPositionerComponent` of `popupAnatomyTests` gets `{ Pending, onOpenChange }`. It returns a modal root that is open at the mount, with a portal and no backdrop part (`VUE-61`). `Pending` is an async component that renders its slot when the suite loads it. Put the positioner and its popup part in `Pending`. Give the positioner the test id `shown`. The suite asserts two things while the positioner has not mounted. No internal backdrop covers the page, and a real press on the page closes the popup with the reason `outside-press`. After the positioner mounts, the suite asserts that the internal backdrop covers the page and that the scroll is locked.
- `createPopup` of `popupAnatomyTests` returns the popup part with `data-testid="popup"`. One case gives it as `content` after the open completes. The suite skips that case when the call gives no `createPopup`. Set `reportsOpen` to `false` for a root that emits only `false`, such as `NavigationMenu.Root`.
- `nextCall(mock)` resolves at the next call of a mock function, such as the listener of `openChangeComplete`. It has no time limit. Call it before the action.
- `mockRuntimeLocale(locale)` makes `Intl.NumberFormat` use `locale` when a call gives no locale, until the test ends. `getFormatter` keeps each formatter by locale and options. Thus format with options that no earlier test of the file formatted with no locale.
- `mockRuntimeDateTimeLocale(locale)` makes `Intl.DateTimeFormat` use `locale` when a call gives no locale, until the test ends. A date part with no `locale` then reads the names, the hour cycle and the week of `locale` after the mount.
- A `.node.test` file imports these functions by a relative path to `test/mockRuntimeLocale`.
- `useTemporalPolyfill()` replaces the global `Temporal` with `temporal-polyfill`, with all its calendars, until the test file ends. A `.node.test` file imports it by a relative path to `test/temporalPolyfill`.
- `internals/date-time/temporal.polyfill.node.test.ts` runs the cases of the `.test` files of the helpers on the polyfill. It calls `useTemporalPolyfill()` and then imports those files with `await import()`. A static import runs before the call, so a case table that reads `Temporal` when its file loads would get the native `Temporal`.
- A `.polyfill.node.test` file does not import a file that has a case with `vi.unstubAllGlobals()`, because that call removes the polyfill. To compare the server HTML of the native `Temporal` with the HTML of the polyfill, the file renders the native HTML before it calls `useTemporalPolyfill()`. `internals/date-time/field/field.polyfill.node.test.tsx` does this.
- The root of each date or time family has such a file beside its `.node.test.tsx` file. `time-clock/root/TimeClockRoot.polyfill.node.test.tsx` is an example.
- `test/server/printSegmentLayouts.ts` makes layouts in a Node.js process with no Vitest. A `.node.test` file starts it with `spawnSync` and gives it the cases as JSON on standard input. When `Intl` ends that process, the case gets the signal and the name of the last case.
- `test/animation.ts` has the helpers for a test of a transition or an animation. `nextEvent(target, type, { after, action })` resolves with the next event of that type. With `after`, it ignores each event of that type that comes before the next event of the type `after`. With `action`, it runs the function after it attached its listeners, and the count of frames starts when the function ends. It rejects when no event comes in 180 frames of the page. `nextAnimationsRead(element, { action })` resolves with the list that the next `getAnimations()` call on the element returns, and it rejects when no call comes in 600 frames of the page. A part makes that call when it starts to wait for the animations of the element. The `action` of `nextAnimationsRead` works as the `action` of `nextEvent`. `nextEndingStyle(element, { action })` resolves in the update that gives the element `data-ending-style`. An attribute that the element has at the call does not resolve it. It rejects when no such update comes in 180 frames of the page. The `action` of `nextEndingStyle` works as the `action` of `nextEvent`. `createTransitionRunCounter(isOpen)` returns `counts` and the handler `onTransitionrun`. The handler counts the `transitionrun` events of the element itself. An event counts as an enter when `isOpen()` is `true` at the event, and as an exit when it is `false`. `probeFrameAfterExit(panel, isGone, close)` calls `close` and waits until the part reads the animations of the panel. Then it moves each animation to 50 ms before its end. It tells if the panel was gone in the frame callback of the frame in which the exit ended. The exit must last 100 s. The probe rejects when the part finds no animation, and when the panel goes before the end of the exit. `probeFrameAfterExitBeforeRead(panel, isGone, close)` calls `close` and waits for the update that gives the panel `data-ending-style`. It rejects when that update does not come in 180 frames of the page. Then it moves each animation of the panel to 1 ms before its end, so the next frame ends the exit. It tells if the panel was gone in the frame callback of that frame, and if a part read the animations of the panel before the end. The exit must last 100 s. The probe rejects when the panel has no animation, and when the panel goes before the end of the exit. It sees only a read with a `getAnimations()` call on the panel itself. `seekBeforeEnd(animations, margin)` moves each animation to `margin` ms before its end, so the next frame ends it. It throws when the list is empty and when an animation lasts less than 10 s, so a case that uses it gives the animation a duration of 100 s. `openInTimerTask(open)` and `closeInTimerTask(open)` change a ref in a timer task. `POPUP_EXIT_CSS` is a style sheet that gives an element of the class `popup` an exit animation of 100 s. Put it in a `<style>` element. Give the popup part `class="popup"`.
- A limit of `waitFor`, of a `findBy` query or of a helper of `test/animation.ts` is a count of frames. It is not a time. A busy machine can stall the page for some seconds. The page renders no frame during the stall, so the stall uses no frame of a limit. The browser ends a transition or an animation only in a frame. A part can move the focus into a popup in a frame.
- `getFocusableUnderAriaHidden()` returns each element of the document that has a `tabIndex` of 0 or more, and that has `aria-hidden="true"` on itself or on an ancestor. The Tab key can put the focus on such an element, and a screen reader does not announce it. The function does not read `disabled` or the layout, so the list can also have a disabled element or an element that is not rendered. In WebKit on an Apple system, a focus guard has no `aria-hidden`, so the list has no guard there.
- `createHeldBranch(renderPart, options)` returns `HeldBranch`, `hide`, `mountWhileHeld`, `show` and `mountInActivation`. `HeldBranch` renders a branch in `KeepAlive`.
- `hide()` hides the branch and flushes. `mountWhileHeld()` calls `hide()`, and then mounts the vnode of `renderPart` in the branch and flushes. The mount thus comes in a later flush than the hide. A part that mounts in the flush of the hide gets `onDeactivated`, so it is not a held mount.
- `show()` shows the branch and flushes. `mountInActivation()` shows the branch and mounts the part in one flush. `KeepAlive` moves the branch into the document before the part mounts, and the part gets its first `onActivated` call in that flush. The branch renders the part from its own state, so the part gets `onActivated` before `onMounted`. `mountInActivation({ fromProp: true })` gives the mount to the branch as a prop. `KeepAlive` then mounts the part when it patches the branch, and the part gets `onMounted` first.
- `options.renderBranch(part)` renders the content of the branch. `part` is the vnode of `renderPart`, or `null` before the mount. The default is a `div` that holds `part`.
- `options.mounted` mounts the part with the branch. The default is `false`.
- A test file does not write a `KeepAlive` branch with a flag for a later mount. A case of a part that mounts in a held branch renders `HeldBranch` in the place of the part. A form that a case needs goes into the helper.
- `useTestInteractions(propsList)` merges the props that the interaction composables return. It returns `getReferenceProps`, `getFloatingProps`, `getItemProps` and `getTriggerProps`.
- An entry of `propsList` is a props object, or a ref or a getter that gives one. A composable can return a ref of its props, and the list takes that ref. Each of the four functions reads the entries when it runs.
- `useFloating(options)` of `test/floating-ui-tests/useFloating.ts` makes the store and the positioning context of a popup in a test. It is not in `#test-utils`.
- `options` has `open` and `placement` (each a value, a ref or a getter), `onOpenChange` and `nodeId`. The result has `refs` and `context`. `refs.reference` and `refs.floating` are the refs of the two elements. `refs.setReference` and `refs.setFloating` write them, and the store follows. `context.rootStore` is the store. The helper computes no position.
- `expectType<Expected, Actual>(value)` makes a type error when `Actual` is not identical to `Expected`. The call then needs a second argument of the type `never`, and TypeScript reports TS2554: `Expected 2 arguments, but got 1`. The helper counts the arguments, because a `never` value is assignable to each parameter type. In a `.ts` or `.tsx` file, put the `@ts-expect-error` of a call that must fail on the line before the name `expectType`.
- `expectNoAxeViolations(element, options)` throws when axe finds a violation in `element`. `options` has the options of `axe.run`, and `accept(rule, element)`. The helper ignores the report of `rule` for each element that `accept` accepts. It still fails for another element of that rule. It reads only the violations of the result, as [Server render](#server-render) says.

The hydration helpers:

- `renderOnServer(file, props)` resolves to the server HTML of a component. `file` is the path of a Vue file from the root of the package, such as `test/fixtures/PlainPart.vue`.
- The render runs in Node with no DOM, as a server makes it. A Vue warning in that render fails the test.
- A composition of parts, or a composable, needs a fixture SFC as `file`. Give it a name that ends in `.test.vue`, and keep it in the folder of its test. Vitest does not run such a file, and the lint rules of the source do not apply to it. Examples are `ProgressRootHydration.test.vue` and `UseIsHydratingProbe.test.vue`.
- A fixture part is a part that tests use in place of a part of a family, such as `CompositeRootPart.vue`. It is an SFC in `packages/vue/test/fixtures`. A composition of parts stays in the folder of its tests, also when more than one test file imports it.
- `test/fixtures/FocusManagerPart.vue` is a focus manager with a store of its own. It renders a `div`, and the guards go beside it. The tests of the portal use it.
- `test/fixtures/floatingFocusManagerHarness.tsx` gives `FloatingFocusManager`, a component over `useFloatingFocusManager` for the ported cases that Base UI writes with `<FloatingFocusManager>`. The first element of its default slot is the element of the composable.
- `test/fixtures/DateTimeFieldProbe.vue` is the test part of the date and time fields. It calls `useDateTimeFieldRoot` for the root and `useDateTimeFieldSegment` for each segment. The tests of `internals/date-time/field` render it.
- `test/fixtures/HydrationFixture.vue`, `StyledElement.vue`, `ClassedElement.vue`, `LabelForControl.vue` and `RemountedRegion.vue` are exceptions. They are the fixtures of `test/hydration.test.tsx`, which tests the hydration helpers. They have no `.test.vue` name, and they are not in the folder of their test. The file is outside `packages/vue/src`, so the lint rules of the source do not apply to it.
- The props go to Node as JSON, so a prop cannot be a function.
- The browser projects share one Node process for `renderOnServer`. A fixture for a server render reads no global.
- To assert on the server HTML before the hydration, parse it in a `<template>` element. That parse has no side effect.
- The DOM matchers refuse an element of that parse, because its document has no window. Read the element with `getAttribute` and `querySelector`.
- `setAttributeInHtml(html, selector, name, value)` of `#test-utils` gives `html` with `value` in the attribute `name` of the first element that matches `selector`. It parses the HTML in a `<template>` element, and it throws when no element matches. A case of a server whose `Intl` data differs from the data of the browser changes the server HTML with it.
- `mockBrowserToday(distance)` makes `Temporal.Now.plainDateISO` give another day until the test ends, and it returns that day. The day differs from today in the day only, in the month or in the year. Call it after `renderOnServer`, because the server render reads the clock of Node. The day is two days, months or years from today, so it also differs from the day of the server across midnight or another time zone.
- `hydrate(component, { serverHtml, props })` hydrates that HTML. The rules below are its contract.
- It inserts the HTML with `Range.createContextualFragment`, because `innerHTML` does not run an inline script.
- It records the Vue warnings of its two apps as `render` does, and it reads the messages that the test setup records. Vue writes some hydration messages only to the console.
- After mount and two ticks, it fails the test on a Vue warning or a console call. It also fails the test when the hydrated DOM differs from a client-only render.
- The comparison ignores comments and the order of the attributes.
- With `renumberIds: true`, the comparison replaces each generated id with a number in the order of its first use. Give it for a part that mounts again after the mount, because Vue gives the new part a new id. An ID reference that names another element still fails.
- It compares the `style` of each element as the browser parses it, so `border:0` is equal to `border: 0px`. An empty `style` or `class` attribute is equal to no attribute.
- `hydrate` mounts the client-only render first, and removes it before it hydrates. The document thus holds one app at a time, as a page does.
- `hydrateAfter(component, { serverHtml, props, before })` inserts the server HTML as `hydrate` does. It then awaits `before(container)`, and it hydrates the HTML. `before` does what a user or a browser does before the scripts of a page run, such as a focus, a key press or a write of `input.value`.
- After mount and two ticks, `hydrateAfter` fails the test on a Vue warning or a console call. It makes no comparison with a client-only render, because `before` changes the DOM.
- `useValueBeforeHydration` sends the `change`, `blur` and `focusout` of a changed control one tick after its `input` event, and the events of a second control come in a later flush. A case that needs a state that these events set waits for that state with `waitFor`, such as `data-touched`.
- The navigation type of the test page depends on how the runner loaded the page. A case of a part that passes `restorable: false` thus calls `mockNavigationType(type)` of `#test-utils`.
- The `load` event of the test page has ended, so a part that mounts in a test does not wait for a `pageshow` event. A case of a render before the restore take calls `mockPageBeforeLoad()` of `#test-utils`. It gives a `back_forward` navigation until the test ends, with a `loadEventEnd` of 0 until the case ends `load`.
- `mockPageBeforeLoad()` returns a function that sets `loadEventEnd` and then sends `pageshow`, in the order of the browser. A case in which `load` ends while `KeepAlive` holds the part calls that function, because the part reads `loadEventEnd` at its activation.
- `recordInputEvents(target)` of `#test-utils` records the type of each `input`, `change`, `blur` and `focusout` event that reaches `target`. A case of a take uses it to assert the events of the take.
- A script that dispatches `pageshow` runs each listener in one task, with no flush between two listeners. A case in which the take of one part renders a later part before the listener of that part is thus a case of `fixtures/vite`, which a real restore of Chromium reaches. A case in which two inputs of one controlled parent take their values in two changes is also such a case. `Slider.Root` takes the inputs of all its thumbs in one change, so a case of a range slider can dispatch `pageshow`.
- To test what an inline script of the server HTML does before the app starts, insert the HTML with a fragment of a range, as `hydrate` does. Make the assertions before a mount. `internals/PrehydrationScript.test.tsx` is the model.
- To test a nonce, load an `<iframe>` whose document has a `Content-Security-Policy` in a `<meta>` element. Insert the server HTML into the document of the frame, and collect its `securitypolicyviolation` events.
- The browser sends each `securitypolicyviolation` event in a later task, in the order of the violations. A wait for animation frames does not wait for that task.
- To show that a part causes no violation, insert a control element with no nonce after the part inserts its elements. Wait for the event of the control with `nextEvent`. Then assert the whole list of events. `scroll-area/root/ScrollAreaRoot.test.tsx`, "sets the nonce, so that a policy that needs the nonce accepts the style element", is the model.
- Collect the events with a capture listener on the document of the frame. Add the listener before the mount of the part. `nextEvent` resolves in the listener of the target, and the case continues before a bubble listener of the document gets the event.

`hydrate` cannot test a part that hydrates after the two ticks, such as an async component in `<Suspense>`. Hydrate such a part by hand, as `progress/value/ProgressValue.test.tsx` does:

1. Put the server HTML in a container in the document, and get the element.
2. Make the app with `createSSRApp(fixture, props)`.
3. Register the unmount of the app and the removal of the container with `onTestFinished`.
4. Mount the app on the container.
5. Call `await waitFor(…)` with one assertion on the new text.
6. Assert that the element is the same node. After a mismatch, Vue discards the server nodes.
7. Assert that `takeReportedMessages()` returns an empty list.

A case that acts between the server render and the hydration calls `hydrateAfter` with the action in `before`. A case that must assert before the two ticks hydrates by hand. An example is a wait for the load of an `<img>` of the server HTML. Such a case does steps 1 to 4. Where Base UI asserts immediately after `hydrate()`, it then calls `await nextTick()` and makes its assertions. Step 7 is its last step. See `avatar/image/AvatarImage.test.tsx`.

### Test rules

- Use only the Vitest APIs: `expect`, the functions of `vi`, such as `vi.fn` and `vi.spyOn`, and the DOM matchers.
- The DOM matchers are the matchers of `@testing-library/jest-dom`, as in Base UI. A ported assertion then has the same meaning.
- `test/setup.ts` loads them only in the browser projects. The matchers use `css.escape`, which defines the global `CSS`. A server has no such global, so a `.node.test` file has no DOM matchers.
- `toHaveTextContent` passes when the text contains the string. To compare the whole text, use `expect(element.textContent).toBe(text)`.
- Give a DOM matcher an element, not a Vitest locator.
- Put one assertion in each `waitFor`.
- In a test of `packages/vue`, do not give a wait a limit in seconds. A busy machine can stall the page for longer than the limit, and a correct part then fails the case. The tests of `fixtures/nuxt` run Playwright from Node, and their waits keep a limit of time.
- In a test of `packages/vue`, use `waitFor`, `screen` and `within` of `#test-utils`. Do not use the waits of `@testing-library/dom`, `vi.waitFor`, `vi.waitUntil`, `expect.poll` or `expect.element`. `pnpm lint` refuses them.
- Do not call a `queryBy` or `queryAllBy` query in the callback of `waitFor`. The absence is also true before the element appears, so the wait can end before the change. `pnpm lint` refuses the call.
- Where the absence is true when the awaited action resolves, assert it directly. Advance the timers or flush first. Assert a state that a timer ends directly after the timer advances. Where a case waits for a first measurement, wait for a value that the measurement writes. Then assert the absence.
- To wait until an element goes, get the element before the action. After the action, call `await waitFor(() => { expect(element).not.toBeInTheDocument(); })`. Then assert the absence with the query.
- A part can remove an element in a timer or in a frame. `flushMicrotasks()` does not wait for a timer or a frame, so wait for the removal with the held element. An example is a popup that closes after `blur()`: the part closes it in a 0 ms timer.
- The action can be in a helper. `closeWith` of `test/detachedTriggersConformanceTests.tsx` calls `blur()` for the focus interaction. Read each awaited helper before you assert an absence directly after it.
- A part can also do other work in a 0 ms timer, such as the validation of `Field.Control` after Enter. `flushMicrotasks()` and a command of `userEvent` of `vitest/browser` do not wait for that timer. Wait for the result of that work with `waitFor`. To assert that the timer did no work, call `await wait(0)` after the action. The browser runs timers of equal delay in the order in which they started.
- To find a case that needs such a wait, wrap `window.setTimeout` in `test/setup.ts`. Add 5 ms to each delay. A message task, such as the task of `flushMicrotasks()`, then runs before a 0 ms timer. A case that then fails needs a wait. Do not commit the wrapper.
- A case that asserts that a timer did no work can pass with a broken part when the timer is late. The wrapper thus cannot find such a case. To check it, change the part so that the timer does the work that it must not do. Run the file of the case with the wrapper. The case must fail. Do not commit the change.
- Count runs, not repeats. Vitest reports a case as failed when one of its repeats fails, so `--repeats` cannot show that a case fails each time. Run the file 10 times. A case that sees the changed part fails in each run.
- One frame is not a wait for a 0 ms timer, because a frame can come before a late timer. An example is a modal popup, which locks the scroll of the page in a 0 ms timer after it opens. To assert that the page has no lock, call `await wait(0)` after the frame. To check a case that waits one frame, make the wrapper add 20 ms, not 5 ms. With 5 ms, the frame can still come after the timer, and the case then sees the work of the timer. A lag of 20 ms is longer than a frame (16.7 ms at 60 Hz). A frame that the case requests soon after the timer starts thus comes first, and the case does not see the work of the timer.
- `useFocus` starts a 0 ms timer when a trigger loses the focus. The timer must not close the popup when the focus moves to another trigger of the same popup or into the popup. A case can move the focus without a call of `focus()`. A hover of a trigger in a menubar moves the focus to that trigger while a menu of the menubar is open. A menu that opens can move the focus from its trigger into its popup. `switchTrigger` of `test/detachedTriggersConformanceTests.tsx` and `activateTrigger` of `test/viewportConformanceTests.tsx` wait for the timer for the focus interaction.
- A negative assertion on an attribute or a value can stay in `waitFor` when the case asserts the start state before the action.
- To wait for a presence, call a `getBy` query in the callback: `await waitFor(() => { expect(screen.getByRole('menu')).not.toBe(null); })`. The query throws while the element is absent.
- A case that tests the observer of the content waits for the first measurement, and then for two animation frames. The first notification of each observer then has come. If the content changes before it, the observer of the viewport measures. The case then does not test the observer of the content. `ScrollAreaContent.test.tsx` is the model.
