- `packages/vue/test/demos.ownVariants.test.tsx` finds each demo of such a component by itself. It mounts the two variants in `DemoFrame`, in the light theme and in the dark theme. It fails when an element has another cursor, text color, background color, border color or placeholder color in one variant.
- The test compares each demo at rest and with `disabled`. For `disabled`, it gives `disabled` to the demo and puts the demo in a disabled `Field.Root`. `disabled` on a `<div>` root reaches no part, but the parts in it read the field. A demo that cannot be disabled is in `DEMOS_WITHOUT_DISABLED_STATE` in the test, with its reason in a comment. For each other demo, the test fails when `disabled` adds no `data-disabled`. For a demo in that set, it fails when `disabled` adds one.
- A state that only a pointer or a layout gives needs a row in `ATTRIBUTE_STATES` in the test. The row has the selector of the part and its data attributes. The test writes each set of these attributes on each part that the selector matches. The row of Resizable has `data-at-min`, `data-at-max` and `data-crossing`.
- A demo that hides a part at rest needs a step in `SHOW_EACH_PART` in the test. The step shows the part.
- The test does not read what paints nothing: the hidden input of a part, the colors of a native checkbox, and the color of a border side that has no width.
- On the page, a demo is inside `<DemoFrame>`. The frame has the class `vp-raw`, so the styles of the site do not reach the demo. Show the Vue file and the style sheet directly below it in one `code-group`. The frame and the code group then make one card.
- A demo that Base UI has in two variants gives its Tailwind component to the slot `#tailwind` of the frame. The import name is the name of the CSS-module import and `Tailwind`, as `DemoButtonHeroTailwind`. The page shows the source of each variant in its own `<DemoSource variant="css-modules">` or `<DemoSource variant="tailwind">`, with one `code-group` in it. `<DemoSource>` shows only the source of the chosen variant. `docs/components/button.md` is the model.
- The frame shows a select with the label "Styling method" only when it has both variants. The choice is for the whole site, and it stays in `localStorage`. The default is CSS Modules.
- A demo declares its props itself, in a type literal: `defineProps<{ as?: string | Component | undefined }>()`. It does not extend a type of `@vase-ui/vue`. The SFC compiler then reads the props from the demo file, and not from a type of the built package.
- A code sample that is not a demo is `docs/components/<family>/<Name>.vue`, or `docs/utils/<family>/<Name>.vue` on a utility page. The page shows its source and does not render it. `pnpm docs:typecheck` checks it.
- The docs package has no router. A sample that imports one, such as `RouterLink` of `vue-router` or `NuxtLink` of Nuxt, is a component of the Nuxt fixture in `fixtures/nuxt/app/components`. The page shows it with `<<< ../../fixtures/nuxt/app/components/<Name>.vue`. The type check of the Nuxt fixture checks it. The link samples of the Navigation Menu page are the examples.
- A page can show a part of a sample. Mark the part with `// #region <name>` and `// #endregion <name>` in the script of the sample. Show it with `<<< ./<family>/<Name>.vue#<name>{ts}`. The `ts` gives it the colors of TypeScript.
- Write `<ApiReference />` under the heading `## API reference`. The site build makes the reference from the types and the JSDoc of the source. The reference lists the parts in the sequence of the Base UI page, which the extract records. Do not write a table by hand. To change a text of the reference, change the JSDoc.
- A Base UI page can end the heading of a part of its API reference with `[New]` or `[Preview]`, as in `### FilterProvider [Preview]`. The extract records the word, and the reference shows it as a badge on the heading of the part. Do not write the word on the page.
- A Base UI page can also end another heading of its API reference with `[New]` or `[Preview]`, such as the heading of a function. The extract records the word. The page of the site writes that heading, so the reference cannot show the badge. The site build and `pnpm test:docs` then fail, and the message says what to do.
- The column Arguments of the table Events shows the arguments of an event as the type literal of `defineEmits` writes them, such as `[open: boolean, eventDetails: DialogRootChangeEventDetails]`. The type text of `vue-component-meta` can lose the name of an alias, or give the name of another alias of the same type.
- The table Events lists the events in the sequence of `defineEmits`, not in the order of `vue-component-meta`.
- The column Slot props of the table Slots lists the props of a slot in the sequence of their declaration. The properties that an `Omit` or a `Pick` copies also keep that sequence when one interface or type literal declares all of them.
- The description of an event is its JSDoc in `defineEmits`. Write a JSDoc for each event, `update:x` included.
- `pnpm test:docs` fails when a prop on a family page has no type. It also fails when an event on a family page has no type or no description.
- `vue-component-meta` leaves out a prop with the `on` name of an emit, such as `onFormSubmit` of `Form`. The reference reads the type and the JSDoc of such a prop from its declaration. [Other callbacks](#other-callbacks) gives that pattern.
- The reference leaves out a prop with the tag `@ignore`, as the Base UI page does. `pnpm parity:check` still reads that prop.
- A component of Vase UI's own has no extract and no Base UI page. Its reference lists the parts, functions and types of its record, each list in the sequence of the record.
- A `types.md` can repeat the parts of another family. The `types.md` of Radio Group repeats `Radio.Root` and `Radio.Indicator`. The reference reads such a part from the entry file of the family that exports it. The page of Radio Group thus shows both parts.
- `<ApiReference name="<name>" />` shows one function, class or type of the family, with no heading for its name and no description. The name is the path of the export, such as `mergeProps`, `Tooltip.Handle`, `useRender.Parameters` or `ToastManagerAddOptions`. A type is an entry of the section API Reference or Additional Types of the `types.md`. The page writes the heading and the text. The build fails when the reference has no function, class or type with that name. For a function or a class, the component writes headings, such as Parameters and Return value. Give it `:heading-level` one more than the level of the heading above it: `<ApiReference name="Dialog.createHandle" :heading-level="3" />` below `## createHandle`. The build fails when such a reference has no `heading-level`. Base UI writes these headings at level 3 in each place.
- When the Base UI page has the section Additional types, write `## Additional types` at the end of the page. Under it, write a `### <name>` heading and `<ApiReference name="<name>" />` for each type that `showAdditionalTypes` of the Base UI page names.
- For a function, the component shows a table of its parameters (`c?` is an optional parameter) and then its return value. For a type, it shows one table of its properties in the sequence of their names, with a column Default when a property has a default. For a union of strings, it shows the values in the sequence of the declaration. For an alias of another union, such as `Calendar.Root.ChangeEventReason`, it shows the values in the sequence of that union.
- A type text writes the members of each union in the sequence of its declaration, and `null` and `undefined` last, as TypeScript does. `priority?: 'low' | 'high'` thus gives `"low" | "high" | undefined`. TypeScript writes the members in the order in which it made their types, and that order changes with the families that a build reads first. The rest of a type text is the text of TypeScript. A union in a function type, an array, a tuple, an intersection or a type argument keeps the text of TypeScript. So does a union whose members the declaration does not name, such as a property of a generic type for one type argument.
- The reference leaves out a type that is an array or a tuple, such as `AccordionValue`, because its properties are those of `Array`. A property with a symbol key has the name that the source writes, such as `[Symbol.iterator]`.
- The reference of a page depends only on its source. `scripts/docs/apiReference.test.ts` reads the family pages in their sequence and in the reverse sequence. It fails when a reference differs.
- For a class, the component shows a table of its public properties and then a table of its public methods, in the sequence of the declaration. A member with the `@internal` tag is not in the tables.
- The column Default shows the text of the `@default` tag in the JSDoc of the prop or the property. For a prop, the reference reads the tag from the exported props interface of the part, such as `TooltipTriggerProps`. It does not show the default of Vue. To show a default, write a `@default` tag.
- The reference reads the tags of a property only from its own declaration. A property that an interface declares again does not get the `@default` tag of the base interface. Write the tag again when the default stays the same.
- Each demo is a test fixture. `packages/vue/test/demos.test.tsx` mounts each demo of both variants, checks it with axe and hydrates its server render, in each engine. It mounts the demo in an element with `data-demo` set to its variant, as the frame does, and it imports `docs/demos/tailwind.css`, so that axe reads the colors that the utilities give. A case of the style sheet checks the scope of `docs/demos/tailwind.css`. The frame of a Tailwind demo gets the utilities and the reset. A portal element gets only the utilities. During a scrub, the cursor of the Tailwind Number Field demo gets the utilities of its classes. The frame of a CSS-module demo gets neither. Inside an element with the class `dark`, a Tailwind demo gets its `dark:` styles. Cases of the frame check the select, the stored choice, the sources and the place of the select. The test also fails when the source of a demo names Base UI or React, or when an `href` in a demo starts with `/`. The file also runs the Promise demo of Toast until its promise rejects, and fails when the browser reports a rejection with no handler. The file also submits the VeeValidate demo and the TanStack Form demo of the Forms page, each below the first demo of the page. It fails when the focus does not go to the first invalid control of the submitted form. The file also mounts the demos of each page in a table together: Toast, Navigation Menu and Forms. It mounts them one time for each styling method, as the page shows them, and checks `document.body` with axe. It fails when two landmarks of one role have the same name. Add a page whose demos have a landmark to that table. A new demo needs no new test.
- The mount case of `demos.test.tsx` mounts each demo closed, so axe and the warning check do not see the content of a popup. `packages/vue/test/demos.popups.test.tsx` has the popup case for this: one case for each demo and variant whose folder names a `Portal` part. The popup case opens each popup of the demo, and each popup that a popup opens, to three levels. It gives the frame and each portal element to axe. A Vue warning fails the case, as it fails each test.
- The popup case mounts the demo in `<DemoFrame>`. It loads the base layer of VitePress, `tokens.css`, `look.css` and `docs/demos/tailwind.css`, so a popup has the styles that the site gives it. The test page has no `postcssIsolateStyles`, so the frame gets the base layer too.
- The popup case checks the open state, not the motion. It turns off the transitions and the animations of the style sheets. After each open, it waits until no element has `data-starting-style` and no animation with an end time runs. axe then reads the colors of the open state.
- The popup case finds a trigger by `aria-haspopup`, or by the attribute of a Tooltip trigger or of a Navigation Menu trigger. `unmarkedTriggers` in that file has a rule for each other trigger. The case fails for a demo that has a `Portal` part and no trigger. Add a rule for a new trigger that has no such attribute. Do not add an attribute to the demo.
- The popup case turns off the axe rule `aria-hidden-focus`. An open popup gives `aria-hidden` to its focus guards. A modal popup, and the list of an input that keeps the focus, also give it to the elements outside the popup. These elements can take the focus. The case thus does not see a demo that writes `aria-hidden` on a control inside a popup. The mount case keeps the rule for the closed demo.
- The popup case accepts three other reports of axe, each for one kind of element and not for the whole rule. `ACCEPTED` in that file has them. A row is for a state that a part sets on purpose. Do not add a row for a fault of a demo. Correct the demo.
- `aria-required-children` is accepted for a menu or a menubar whose only `role="button"` descendants are focus guards. In WebKit on an Apple system, a focus guard is a button. A menu has the guards of its submenu triggers as children, and a menubar has the guards of its menus.
- `aria-allowed-attr` is accepted for an element that has `role="menuitem"` and `aria-selected="true"`. In WebKit, VoiceOver needs the attribute on the highlighted item of a menu that has an input.
- `scrollable-region-focusable` is accepted for an element inside a `role="listbox"`. The arrow keys of the input move the highlight of the list, and the list scrolls to it.
- The popup case does not open a popup whose trigger has no attribute that the case knows and no rule in `unmarkedTriggers`, and it does not fail for such a popup. It runs in the light theme, as the mount case does.

### Thanks page

- `docs/thanks.md` includes the region `thanks` of `THANKS.md`.
- `pnpm thanks:render` writes `THANKS.md`. Do not edit the file.
- A name in `THANKS.md` is data. The owner of an account controls a display name. The author of a commit controls the name of a person with no linked account. The include puts each name into the page with no component, and VitePress compiles the page as a Vue template.
- Two guards make sure that the page has only the text that the script writes. The first is the test of each name in the script. The second is `pnpm test:thanks`.
- The script writes a display name only when `isDisplayName` of `scripts/thanks/render.ts` accepts it. The name must match the pattern `DISPLAY_NAME`, with at most 100 characters and at most three combining marks in a row. For each other name, the script writes the account name.
- A person with no linked account has no account name, and the name is plain text, not the text of a link. The script lists that person only when `isPlainText` accepts the name. `isDisplayName` must accept it, and each dot must follow a letter and precede a space or the end of the name. The script prints the number of names that it does not list. It does not keep the e-mail address that GitHub gives for that person.
- `pnpm test:thanks` writes the file again from the date, the commits and the people that it holds. It fails when the two texts differ. A list item that is not a profile link counts as a name with no link. `renderThanks` refuses such a name when `isPlainText` does not accept it.
- After a change of the fixed text in `scripts/thanks/render.ts`, run `pnpm thanks:render` and commit `THANKS.md` in the same pull request. Until then, `pnpm test:thanks` fails, because `THANKS.md` differs from the text that the script writes.
- Run `pnpm thanks:render` before each release, and commit `THANKS.md`.

## Releases

The workflow `.github/workflows/release.yml` stages each release of `@vase-ui/vue` on npm. The repository holds no npm token.

- A version is `YYYY.M.MICRO`, such as `2026.12.0`. The month has no leading zero, and `MICRO` starts at 0.
- The tag of a release is `v` plus the version: `v2026.12.0`. A ruleset lets only an organization admin create, change or delete a `v*` tag.
- Each release has the dist-tag `latest`. A breaking change ships only in a `YYYY.1.0` release.
- The trusted publisher of the package on npmjs.com names the file `release.yml` and the environment `npm`. npm refuses the release after a change of either name.
- The trusted publisher allows `npm stage publish` only. Do not give it `--allow-publish`. Each step of the job `stage` can get an OIDC token. With `--allow-publish`, each of these steps can make a version public with no approval.
- The workflow writes no release notes and makes no GitHub Release. `CHANGELOG.md` holds the notes of each release, one section for each version. The GitHub Release of a tag has the same text as its section.
- A maintainer publishes the first version of a package by hand, because npm sets a trusted publisher only for a package that exists.

The workflow runs on a push of a `v*` tag. It has two jobs:

- `check` fails when the tagged commit is not on `main`, or when `pnpm release:check-tag` fails. Then it runs the commands of the job `check` of CI. Change the two lists together.
- `stage` installs `@vase-ui/vue` and the workspace root, with `--filter @vase-ui/vue`. The install step can get an OIDC token. The filter adds no dependency or script of another workspace project. The exception is the optional peers that pnpm resolves for `@vase-ui/vue` from those projects, such as `esbuild`. Keep the filter when you add a workspace project. Then `stage` runs `npm stage publish` in `packages/vue`. npm trusted publishing gives it access. A staged version is not public.

To make a release:

1. Do the step that [Thanks page](#thanks-page) gives for a release.
2. Merge a pull request that changes `version` in `packages/vue/package.json` and adds the section of that version to `CHANGELOG.md`.
3. Make sure that the check `required` passed on that commit of `main`. [Pull requests](#pull-requests) says which run gives the commit its checks. The workflow does not run `pnpm test`.
4. As an organization admin, push the tag of that commit: `git tag v2026.12.0 <commit>`, then `git push origin v2026.12.0`.
5. On npmjs.com, approve the staged version with two-factor authentication. Only a maintainer of the package can do this. The version is then public. When more than one version is staged, approve them in order, from the lowest version to the highest.
6. Make the GitHub Release of the tag. Its text is the section of that version in `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Text

- Write commit messages, pull requests, code comments and this file in Simplified Technical English (ASD-STE100).
- Use short sentences, the active voice and one instruction in each sentence.
- Give a thing the name of what it is. Do not use metaphors in names.
- Do not name a GitHub account with the at sign in a file or a pull request. GitHub sends a notification to an account that a pull request names in that form. Use a link to the profile, as `THANKS.md` does.

### Voice

The text that users read has a voice: the README, the site, the documentation and the release notes.

- Dry and short. At most one light line on a page, at the top. Then plain instructions.
- The image is ours: an unstyled library is an empty vase, and you bring the flowers. Use it rarely: the tagline, the home page, the 404 page, release titles.
- Shade is welcome when it aims at the React ecosystem as a whole: it gets every good library first, and Vue waits.
- Shade never aims at a named person, at the quality of a project, at users or at disability. React, Base UI and every other library are included.
- Love goes to the engineers, by name, in the Thanks section.
- A joke never carries a fact. If the line is removed, the page still says everything.
- No wit and no shade in an error or warning message, an accessibility note, an API reference, a migration step or a security note. The same is true for a component page and for all text that a user reads while something is broken.
- No emoji. No exclamation marks.

Welcome:

- "Base UI, arranged for Vue."
- "Vase UI ships no styles. A vase should not pick your flowers."
- "React gets the good component libraries first. Vue gets to wait. We stopped waiting."
- In release notes: "React had this first. Vue has it now. The usual order, at an unusual speed."
- A release title: "2026.12.0: Dialogs, and what hides behind them."
- The 404 page: "Nothing here. Some vases are empty."

Not welcome:

- "Oops! Your Dialog lost its Root". Write the error message of the example in "Error messages".
- A line about a person, or about how good another project is.
